2026-10-09 按所有者“为什么每个命令都还要我审核”反馈，修正策略 v2 的过度拦截。
此前所有 PowerShell 包装在模型审核前被视为不透明操作，输入 token 预算又按字节
检查；现对实际 Windows CMD 下的可验证简单查询和单个普通相对文件的字面
Set-Content 建立有限语法，查询可直接通过，写入需独立模型审核与真实用户授权，
并保证最低 medium 风险。编码、动态表达式、复合脚本、设备名、敏感目标、越界
路径等仍人工或拒绝；不能借模型判断降低本地风险底线。助手优先使用简单 CMD
命令，固定 Pi 文本估算配合保守 UTF-8 上界核对完整的 8,192-token 审核输入。
真实测试同时发现并修正 CMD argv 的反斜杠引号转义问题，执行使用明确外层引号
与 Windows verbatim argv，审核事实一致。策略版本变化使旧绑定不能用于新执行。
新增 12 项回归；集中审核/上下文/Pi 连续执行 58 项通过，真实 Windows 进程验证
PowerShell 查询、文件写入、后续查询三步无需审批且文件确实存在。

查询和字面文件写入；最后删除才提示。构建、Level 1 架构（425 modules / 1,506

2026-10-09 按所有者批准的 ADR-030 计划实现工作模式安全命令自动审核与 Pi 连续执行。
严格白名单直接通过；其他命令用当前模型独立、无工具审核，有限且明确授权的可逆修改
可自动执行，危险操作仍需原单次人工审批，批准后继续任务。安全拒绝、取消、过期、
目标关闭或预算耗尽终止整个循环。聊天、解释/生成技能和 MCP 保持原权限范围。
任务上限为 50 条尝试、30 分钟实际运行（人工等待暂停），最多 4 个并行任务、
每终端一个；单条仍为 30 秒/128 KiB。SQLite migration 42、审核/步骤/批准来源、
经过 Contract 校验的 ToolCall SSE、execution/review 用量与工具卡片已接入。
SSH 决定额外绑定实际主机、用户名及连接执行代次，同 ID 重连也不能使用旧决定。
固定纳入 pi-auto-review 三个纯模块与 MIT 许可，仅经私有 Pi 引擎编译；
不升级固定 Pi，不加载扩展或完整权限系统。来源、许可、客户端与文档已同步。
实现及实际验收边界见[本轮证据](evidence/pi-engine/2026-10-09-auto-review.md)。

另核对 SSH 同 ID 执行代次变化、旧审批兼容、文案与源码快照。真实本地进程、
真实 SSH 协议/进程夹具、Electron source 与最终隔离 Windows 目录包流程通过。

2026-10-09 按所有者要求在当前 Windows x64 检出打包 0.10.0。初始化固定 Pi
子模块并完成冻结依赖安装；Windows Git 的自动 CRLF 转换使模型目录哈希不符，
已将检出文本恢复为索引中的 LF 字节，本地 `core.autocrlf=false`。首次检查发现
传输 ownership store 的 POSIX mode 校验阻止 Windows Runtime 启动；现仅在
POSIX 校验 group/other 权限位，Windows 沿用应用数据目录的 ACL，保留普通文件、
symlink、单硬链接及适用的 owner 校验。新增 store 硬链接拒绝和 POSIX 私有权限
回归；Windows 定向启动/重开/硬链接拒绝 3/3 通过，Runtime 历史与协议所有权
流程通过。POSIX 权限新增用例在 Windows 按平台条件跳过，不宣称已在 Mac/Linux
复验。`bun run build` 和打包 wrapper 返回 0，Level 1 架构（421 模块 / 1,493

当作启动失败，弹出原生阻塞提示。现在只在窗口初始加载和 Runtime 恢复加载
的 catch 中过滤这一取消错误；真正网络/未知加载错误，以及 Runtime 启动、
重启耗尽错误仍走原有失败提示。七项单元覆盖取消、连接拒绝和未知错误。
真实 Electron 回归延迟新窗口的首个文档请求并替换导航，确认实际取消码为
`-3`、无原生错误提示、两窗口 Runtime ready 且新窗口终端连接正常；测试捕获
原生提示避免回归失败时阻塞用户桌面，不改生产提示和 CSP。

## Completed goal: commercial detachment; publication not attempted — 2026-09-26

The owner narrowed this task to commercial detachment from the retired reference
product. The clean local Git root and Mac engineering candidate already establish
technical/source-name separation; signing, notarization, a public download,
remote Git hosting, public support channels and Windows/Linux native tests are
**not completion conditions for this goal**. The MCR-01～04 release gate remains
truthfully incomplete for a later publication task and must not be used as this
goal's progress percentage.

The detachment decision was narrower: verify that retained first-party
code, four-language copy, themes and AX mark are independently authored or
authorized for Axterm's Apache-2.0 commercial use; resolve the documented
source-lineage candidates by retaining with an applicable notice, replacing,
or excluding exact bytes; and keep licenses/notices for separately licensed
third-party components. Zero retired-name matches, a new Git history, and
passing engineering tests are necessary evidence but do not prove these rights.
The owner confirmed in this task on 2026-09-26 that both historical contributor
aliases are their own or authorized contributions, and that the current AX mark,
four-language copy and four terminal palettes were created by them or are
authorized for Apache-2.0 commercial use. The [fixed-reference candidate
review](evidence/COMMERCIAL-DETACHMENT-SOURCE-TRIAGE-2026-09-26.md) records
engineering retain/replace decisions for the 24 matching source-file pairs;
the last long identical monitor collector line was replaced. This owner
attestation and bounded review support closing **this commercial-detachment
engineering goal**, not a copyright non-infringement warranty, trademark
clearance or the later publication's qualified rights signoff. Do not expand
this queue to the 46-item full release board or require a signed package.

Current source passed `bun run check`: 1,220 unit/integration tests and 21
visual/accessibility tests, plus architecture, Contract, source, license and
build checks. The current unsigned Mac app passed the path/byte retired-name
scan and two package legal/About tests; `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`d9d18b28e31c70c5cc20e4f7b2d3eb16361ecd87628fbaf9fc5a7913873441d0`.
Signing, notarization, public download, remote Git hosting and Windows/Linux
native tests remain outside this goal and are not claimed complete.

2026-09-26 follow-up: the old working tree's retired-reference submodule
directory was already absent, and its remaining `vendor/README.md` has now
been removed. The independent Git root never contained `vendor/`. A rights
recheck found that the separately published `trzsz2` library derives from
`trzsz.js`. Its original Lonny Wong MIT copyright and byte-matched license
are now retained in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` and
`licenses/trzsz-js-LICENSE.txt`; the rebuilt unsigned Mac DMG/ZIP and
directory app contain both. `bun run check` passed (1,220 unit/integration
tests, 21 visual/accessibility tests), and the DMG verify/mount/copy/install
journey passed 36 packaged tests with 19 platform/fixture-conditional skips.
The DMG SHA-256 is
`3ea6fdcade713537e55940a9f877f4027db75737f4d13752b97379f353a81076`;
the ZIP SHA-256 is
`5483885579b0811a0df21c17cf31772a77e9bb2a22acc2ab8e4854dcc40e2b6f`.
This closes that specific notice omission, not the later full-publication
legal review.

## Current local engineering candidate — 2026-09-26

The owner approved [ADR-023](../adr/ADR-023-clean-local-source-root-and-data-profile.md):
the unpublished prototype's SQLite/Vault profile is no longer upgraded in place.
The desktop now uses `data-v2/` and `vault-v2/`, leaving the previous directories
untouched. A direct Runtime caller attempting an earlier database receives a
read-only preflight error before migration. Existing Axterm configuration
snapshots remain importable; passwords and images require manual re-entry.

The separate local source tree has been mechanically cleaned and independently
checked for the retired product name in paths, source bytes, generated bundles,
and the unsigned Mac app/DMG/ZIP. The exact copyright lines for historical
source-review candidates remain in legal notices; source-rights review is
**still pending**. The old repository's Git history and previously frozen
candidate remain controlled backups; the old working tree's `vendor/` directory
was later removed. No remote repository, signing, notarization, or
public release has occurred. The four conditions in
[MINIMUM_COMMERCIAL_RELEASE](MINIMUM_COMMERCIAL_RELEASE.md) remain unchecked.
Windows/Linux native verification belongs to the owner and remains pending.

For this clean-root candidate, `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, `bun run check`
(1,213 unit/integration passed; 21 visual/accessibility passed), source-name
audit, unsigned Mac packaging, source and app byte scans, complete DMG journey
(36 passed, 19 explicitly skipped), and controlled SSH/SFTP journey passed.
See [local evidence](evidence/LOCAL-CLEAN-ROOT-MAC-2026-09-26.md). The earlier
failing DMG/SSH runs were fixed and rerun; only the final passing runs count.

2026-09-26 source-rights detachment work is now in progress on top of that
frozen candidate. A bounded comparison against the controlled original found
a closely corresponding Runtime network collector and CPU/memory parsing
expression. The [targeted replacement](evidence/IR02-RUNTIME-MONITOR-SOURCE-REPLACEMENT-2026-09-26.md)
uses Linux `/proc` and `ip` data through an Axterm-defined parser; its focused
tests, typecheck and real Linux-container command check passed. This does not
turn the earlier Mac package into a package of the changed bytes, or mark
source-rights review complete. Exact source, contributor, language, mark and
third-party decisions remain pending; MCR-01～04 stay unchecked.

The scoped `release:minimum-commercial:check` now matches the four-condition
Mac/manual-update completion line without weakening the old full-platform
`release:final-public:check`. Its eight automated hygiene checks pass, while
MCR-01～04 correctly remain blocked by missing named rights, signed/notarized
and public-download, public-policy, and owner-acceptance evidence. The second
local commit also passed frozen installation, full check, DMG installation
(36 passed, 19 conditional skips) and packaged SSH from a separate clean
checkout. Both Mac builds are unsigned and are not formal release evidence.

## Independent-release transition

2026-09-26 商业发行任务范围按所有者最新指示调整：依据
[ADR-022](../adr/ADR-022-commercial-release-mac-acceptance.md) 和
[MCR-01～04](MINIMUM_COMMERCIAL_RELEASE.md)，产品继续以 Mac、Windows、Linux
兼容为目标；本任务只按四条完成线处理实际分发的权利/通知、Mac 正式包、
公开下载/联系渠道与新仓库。**四条通过且所有者一次确认后，标记本任务商业发行完成。**
Windows/Linux 原生测试由所有者在对应电脑自行完成，不阻断本任务，但尚无结果时
必须明示“所有者自测待完成/未验证”，绝不记为已通过。此处是验收政策调整，
**不是当前已可商业发行的声明**；W/IR 计数不变。2026-09-26 实跑
`release:final-public:check` 仍为 14/23 前置通过、9/23 阻断，旧脚本尚未支持
收窄后的 Mac 验收/手动更新门禁；**九项旧阻断不是九个本任务新工作包**，
不能把 `pending` 假填通过。已识别的实际权利/通知问题、Mac 签名公证、
公开下载和最终新仓库仍未完成，本轮只修改文档。

等同于旧运行时入口仍存在，也不能把产品入口移除等同于来源权利审定。

`d6fbc192031fc6f5a4767e59e590155798f7649fc13efda9206c05de73cfdea3`），

发现、2,196 次文本命中**。按文件分组：文档 80、测试 34、packages 28、
scripts 9、apps 8、根文件 7；同一文件可有路径与内容两条发现。
这是预演，不是正式 `release:final-public:check`：公开迁移期及审查前置门禁
仍未满足。M3 到期后须分别处理兼容实现/测试、历史文档的受控归档或逐文件
批准例外，以及通用构建工具中的旧名称；不能以机械改名或提前删除用户迁移
路径代替。**0 W、0 IR、0 M1** 新关闭，计数仍为 **4/46、2/14、14/14**。

W-03-02/T2's ZMODEM finalizer now clears active state and emits
`session-end` even when cleanup fails; it reports a path-free error, prevents
unsafe reuse and lets Runtime destruction retry the failed staged name or
upload source-handle close. The receive file marks its handle closed only
after a successful close. [Focused evidence and current Mac package
hash](evidence/IR03-RUNTIME-GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN-2026-09-25.md) cover **29
tests** with one conditional skip, typecheck, and **1/1** real-PTY packaged
ZMODEM upload/download. The final `bun run check` passed **1,242
unit/integration tests** (38 skipped), all architecture/Contract/source/license/build
gates, and **21/21** visual/accessibility journeys. T1 ownership/re-Grant policy, hard-kill recovery and
T3 real-volume acceptance remain open; totals stay **3/46 W closed, 2/14 IR
Accepted, 11/14 M1 Mac cells passed**.

W-03-02/T2's XMODEM cancellation and asynchronous-publication cleanup no
longer treat a denied removal of the session's own `.part` as success.
It retries that exact name, emits a path-redacted failure, suppresses the EOT
success ACK if a published stage remains, and propagates a rejected cleanup
promise through Runtime-wide close. Five new focused regression cases passed;
the two affected suites passed **30 tests** with one conditional skip, and a
fresh unsigned Mac app passed **1/1** real-PTY XMODEM binary upload/download.
[Current source/package hashes and test limits](evidence/IR03-RUNTIME-GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN-2026-09-25.md)
are recorded. The final `bun run check` passed **1,238 unit/integration
tests** (38 skipped), all architecture/Contract/source/license/build gates
and **21/21** visual/accessibility journeys. Two large-file test fixtures
now seed through the production batch path instead of repeatedly parsing a
growing tree; their >8-MiB roundtrip and >16-MiB rejection assertions stay.
This is T2 partial progress only; T1 ownership/re-Grant,
hard-kill recovery and T3 real-volume acceptance remain open. Totals stay
**3/46 W closed, 2/14 IR Accepted, 11/14 M1 Mac cells passed**.

W-03-02/T2 now also tracks XMODEM's in-flight asynchronous EOT publication
through `destroy()` and `XmodemManager.destroySession()`, so Runtime-wide
cleanup cannot complete before a publication already in progress settles.

a fresh unsigned Mac package passed **1/1** real-PTY XMODEM binary
upload/download. [Source and package hashes, test scope and remaining

W-03-02/T2's independent ZMODEM adapter now limits queued peer input to
**4 MiB and 4,096 chunks** before copying an active chunk. A real-handshake
flood regression confirms one error and cancellation; another regression
confirms ordinary output cannot pin a large buffer through the 17-byte
header tail; an oversized initial frame also stops before arming an idle
timer. ZMODEM's own `.part` removal failures are now path-redacted and
propagated rather than silently treated as cleanup success; injected
`EACCES` cancellation/post-publication tests verify retry and preserved final
bytes. [Focused evidence](evidence/IR03-RUNTIME-GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN-2026-09-25.md)
passes **25 tests** with one conditional skip and typecheck. The final-source
`bun run check` passed **1,231 unit/integration tests** (38 skipped), its
architecture/Contract/source/license/build gates and **21/21**
visual/accessibility journeys. A final-guard unsigned Mac package passed **1/1**
real-PTY ZMODEM binary upload/download; its `app.asar` SHA-256 and scope are
in the linked evidence. This is T2 partial progress, not T1 policy approval
or hard-kill recovery. W-03-02 and IR-03 remain open; totals stay **3/46 W
closed, 2/14 IR Accepted, 11/14 M1 Mac cells passed**.

W-03-02/T2 has another policy-independent Mac safety advance: `trzsz2` now
bounds pending and partially unread peer input at **64 MiB / 4,096 chunks** and reclaims
consumed receive-queue slots. Two direct-library regressions failed before
the patch and pass after it. Runtime catches a queue-overflow rejection,
reports one path-free transfer failure and cancels the session without
throwing into terminal output handling. Focused tests passed **62** with one
conditional skip. A fresh unsigned Mac package passed **6/6** relevant
transfer and legal-notice journeys; hashes, precise coverage and remaining
limits are in the [IR-03 queue follow-up](evidence/IR03-RUNTIME-GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN-2026-09-25.md).
This removes the known unbounded TRZSZ queue gap but **does not accept T2**:
T1's owner/security policy for trusted `.part` ownership, File Grant
re-authorization and crash recovery is still pending, as is T3 real-volume
verification. The current patch's isolated publisher-source rebuild now
matches all 33 installed distribution files; independent rights/protocol
review remains open. Counts stay **3/46 W
closed, 2/14 IR Accepted, 11/14 M1 Mac cells passed**.

The current-source full `bun run check` passed **1,226 unit/integration tests**
(38 skipped), architecture/Contract/source/license/build gates and **21/21**

W-03-02 terminal-transfer cleanup now has a policy-independent TRZSZ safety
correction. Cancellation and post-publication finalization no longer swallow
a failed removal of the writer's own `.part` name. The writer retries that
same name on later cleanup, never deletes the published destination, and
returns a path-redacted failure to Runtime shutdown if cleanup still fails.
`TrzszSession.destroy()` waits for the finalizer and now rejects on that
failure instead of reporting completed cleanup; a live session emits only a
generic error, not the raw filesystem path. Two focused test files passed
**21/21** with one conditional skip, including injected `EACCES`, retry,
preserved final bytes and destruction propagation. This is **T2 partial
progress**, not T1's owner/security policy decision or T2/T3 acceptance;
W-03-02 remains open and the W/IR/M1 totals do not change.

The complete `bun run check` passed **1,213 unit/integration tests** (38
skipped) and **21/21 visual/accessibility tests**. A new isolated unsigned
Mac package passed **2/2** focused real-PTY TRZSZ roundtrip and active-receive
graceful-quit journeys; its hash and scope are recorded in the
[IR-03 follow-up evidence](evidence/IR03-RUNTIME-GRACEFUL-SHUTDOWN-2026-09-25.md).

The next T2 boundary test exposed a real flaw: a peer announcing one byte
could send four and still cause publication. A real TRZSZ handshake now proves
the revised progress guard rejects those bytes, reports failure and removes
staging. After an explicit rebuild, the same unsigned Mac package passed
**3/3** focused roundtrip, declared-size rejection and graceful-quit journeys;
the package/source hashes and one transient roundtrip timeout before the
successful joint rerun are recorded in the same IR-03 evidence. T2 remains
partial; T1/T3 and all W/IR/M1 counts stay unchanged.

The next T2 check found that `trzsz2` accepted an arbitrarily large binary
`#DATA` length before allocating. Its pinned MIT dependency patch now rejects
nonpositive, non-integer or over-16-MiB binary chunks before allocation. The
direct-library regression failed before the patch and passes after it; four
focused TRZSZ suites passed **27 tests** (one conditional skip). A freshly
built and packaged Mac app then passed **4/4** related real-PTY journeys on
one artifact, including malicious chunk-header rejection; see the same IR-03
record for hashes. At that checkpoint, text-mode line bounds and hard-kill
recovery still needed work. This was T2 partial progress only; **0 W, 0 IR and
0 M1 cells** closed.

The final `bun run check` for this source passed **1,215 unit/integration
tests** (38 skipped), the architecture/Contract/source/license/build gates,
and **21/21 visual/accessibility tests**.

in both POSIX and Windows parsing branches. Oversized-line regressions failed

below is historical and its statuses are not the new release gate.

P-03/IR-04's [macOS FTP long-transfer follow-up](evidence/IR04-MACOS-FTP-LONG-TRANSFER-2026-09-25.md)
corrects the control socket's 30-second idle timeout interrupting an active
data transfer. Real-TCP 150 ms timeout regressions verify continued upload
and slow download, final replies, `NOOP` and resumed idle closure; the FTP
Adapter suite passes 32/32. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory package copied outside the
checkout passed the FTP Widget journey 1/1 with a 32-second streamed upload,
exact bytes and same-session `NOOP`. Its concrete Resources matched the
generated SPDX sidecar. A subsequent
[packaged slow-download follow-up](evidence/IR04-MACOS-FTP-SLOW-DOWNLOAD-2026-09-25.md)
passed the opt-in journey **1/1** using a 36 MiB curl download limited to
1 MiB/s: elapsed time exceeded 30 seconds, source/download SHA-256 matched,
and a post-transfer `NOOP` received `200` on the same control connection.
The selected unsigned macOS package's Resources again matched its SPDX
sidecar. Other data-stall cases, Windows/native-Linux installed proof,
independent protocol/security and rights review remain open; IR-04 stays **In
progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

P-03/IR-04's [packaged stalled-data follow-up](evidence/IR04-MACOS-FTP-STALLED-DATA-2026-09-25.md)
now covers a passive upload that sends partial bytes and then stops. The
opt-in unsigned macOS arm64 packaged journey passed **1/1**: after the
approximately 30-second data timeout, the existing target remained intact,
the staging file disappeared and a fresh authenticated FTP session answered
`NOOP`. The `basic-ftp` client closes its own control connection on premature
data-stream closure, so same-client recovery and a surfaced `426` are **not**
claimed. The selected package's Resources matched its SPDX sidecar. Other
transfer failure modes, independent protocol/security and rights review, Windows/native-Linux
installed proof and signed distribution remain open; IR-04 stays **In
progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

P-03/IR-04's [active-mode stall follow-up](evidence/IR04-MACOS-FTP-ACTIVE-STALL-2026-09-25.md)
then added a 300 ms real-TCP timeout regression: after partial bytes in `PORT`
mode, the server returns `426`, removes staging, preserves the existing file
and accepts `NOOP` on the same control connection. The focused Adapter file
passes **33/33**. A directory-package trial passed the opt-in active-stall
journey **1/1**, but packaging preceded a fresh TypeScript/Electron build;
that trial does not prove current-source package identity. The later
[rebuilt DMG independent-install journey](evidence/IR13-MACOS-CURRENT-DMG-INSTALL-2026-09-25.md)
passes the same active stall from the installed copy. Independent
protocol/security, source-rights, native-platform, signing and release
acceptance are still pending; IR-04 remains **In progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

P-06's migration release-record gate now rejects an approval dated after the
public migration release or in the future, non-stable version strings, a
stable milestone at or before the migration release, and a compatibility-
removal version that is not later than every milestone. Build metadata cannot
make two versions count as separate stable releases. The focused record suite
passes 6/6; the actual record remains `pending` and no migration clock or
removal approval has been asserted. IR-08 and IR-09 remain unaccepted.

upgrade or migration-release approval; IR-08 remains **In progress**.

P-01/IR-02 now also has a generated per-file source-review handoff:

file bytes; the ordinary `check` rejects stale scope, and the final-public

### Commercialization-stage tracker

The plan has a preparation **stage 0** plus five execution stages (**1–5**).
The current engineering focus is **stage 3 — compatibility migration and Axterm
UI/UX**. This is a work-order marker, not a claim that earlier exit gates have
been accepted: stages 0–2 have their decisions, ADR and replacement work under
way, but their source-rights, human-review and cross-platform evidence remains
open. Stage 4 has P-08 evidence in progress in parallel; stage 5 cannot begin
formal new-repository acceptance until the prior release gates are met.

| Plan stage                               | Current position                                                                        | Why it is not marked complete                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 0 — inventory and decisions              | Product choices are recorded; audit records exist.                                      | Complete per-file/package rights, contributor, brand and retained-asset review remains needed.                  |
| 1 — specification                        | ADR-016 and the independent matrix control delivery.                                    | The dependent release-evidence rows remain unaccepted.                                                          |
| 2 — runtime/data replacement             | Protocol, FTP, locales, themes and asset implementation changed substantially.          | Source-rights/human review, upgrade and Windows/Linux evidence remain.                                          |
| 3 — migration and UI/UX                  | **Active.** P-06 migration containment and P-07 visual/accessibility work are underway. | Public stable migration release/date/window, recovery and reviewed cross-platform UX evidence remain.           |
| 4 — licenses, supply chain and artifacts | P-08 notices/SBOM/snapshot work is underway in parallel.                                | Complete notices/rights review, signing, updater and three-platform package evidence remain.                    |
| 5 — clean checkout and new repository    | Not started as a formal acceptance stage.                                               | It requires the preceding release gates; new Git repository creation and old-GitHub deletion are owner actions. |

| Work package                              | State       | Next evidence                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| P-01 Product decision and governance      | In progress | IR-01 accepted; IR-02 still needs contributor/source-rights and retained-asset review                                          |
| P-02 Terminal-transfer source replacement | In progress | Independent SSH review, Windows/native-Linux transfer, network/removable-filesystem portability, source-rights review          |
| P-03 FTP server replacement               | In progress | Independent protocol/security review plus Windows and native-Linux packaged evidence                                           |
| P-04 Four independent locales             | In progress | Human language/rights review, full visible-copy coverage and Windows/native-Linux package evidence                             |
| P-05 Curated themes and asset rights      | In progress | Packaged upgrade, visual/accessibility and asset/license review; migration-release notice                                      |
| P-06 Compatibility migration              | In progress | Public migration release/date/window, preserved user-created old-version artifacts, recovery and Windows/native-Linux evidence |
| P-07 Axterm UI/UX and test baseline       | In progress | Reviewed Shell/Settings/dialog design, contrast and packaged/cross-platform visual evidence                                    |
| P-08 Notices and release                  | In progress | IR-11 npm/Rust source and binary notice audit; IR-13 signed, installed and upgrade evidence across supported platforms         |

P-02/IR-02's transfer-library provenance handoff now identifies the official
npm `gitHead` commits for exact `zmodem2@1.4.0` and `trzsz2@1.2.0` versions;
the installed LICENSE/README files match those immutable publisher commits.

development dependency, absent from Axterm's frozen lockfile. A local test
pins the installed metadata, notices and this distinction. Neither GitHub
commit contains the published `dist/` bundle, and no qualified code-lineage or
rights conclusion follows from the metadata match. The installed CJS/ESM
source maps now link eleven embedded executable TypeScript files byte-for-byte
to those immutable commits; [the hash record](evidence/IR02-TRANSFER-SOURCE-MAP-2026-09-24.json)
is checked offline. A separate, isolated [Linux arm64 rebuild](evidence/IR02-TRANSFER-REPRODUCIBLE-BUILD-2026-09-24.md)
reproduced both entire installed `dist/` trees exactly after applying the
declared `trzsz2` patch. This proves local distribution-file reproducibility,
not original authorship or legal provenance. A [fixed-reference comparison](evidence/IR02-TRANSFER-LEGACY_PROTOTYPE-REFERENCE-2026-09-24.md)
also confirms that the historical Legacy Prototype commit consumes those **same two
library versions**; separately published packages are not proof of an absent
ecosystem relationship. The focused
third-party source suite passes 31/31, and `bun run check` passes 222 test files (1,131
passed, 33 skipped), 359 architecture modules / 1,310 dependencies, all 267
Contract operations, build/package layout and 11 visual/accessibility journeys.
IR-02 and IR-03 remain
**In progress**; see the [publisher-source record](evidence/IR02-TRANSFER-PUBLISHER-SOURCE-2026-09-24.md).

P-02/IR-03 now has real `zmodem2` and `trzsz2` protocol-pair regressions for a
remote `../outside.bin` offer: the outside file remains unchanged, received
bytes stay under the selected directory with a sanitized leaf name, and
events expose no absolute path. The focused files pass 30/30. The
[filename-confinement record](evidence/IR03-TRANSFER-FILENAME-CONFINEMENT-2026-09-24.md)
recorded that the former unsupported-hard-link `COPYFILE_EXCL` fallback was
not guaranteed atomic. The native worktree trial below removes that fallback,
but real-filesystem portability remains unaccepted. IR-03 is **In progress**.

P-02's former non-atomic `COPYFILE_EXCL` fallback has a concrete candidate
resolution rather than a presumed equivalence to hard-link publication:
[ADR-019](../adr/ADR-019-atomic-no-replace-transfer-publication.md) is
**Proposed**, and an [isolated macOS arm64 proof of concept](evidence/IR03-ATOMIC-PUBLICATION-NATIVE-POC-2026-09-24.md)
verified native no-replace move success, collision preservation and fail-closed
behavior when native support is disabled. A later macOS worktree trial now
pins and packages the candidate; no real hard-link-limited volume or release
adoption has been accepted. The
native provider's rights/notice, Headless/Electron and three-platform evidence
remain prerequisites; IR-03 is **In progress**.

The public `publishFileExclusive({ strategy: 'rename-noreplace' })` API of that
exact candidate was also tested in isolation: a successful macOS arm64 call
returned a synced directory receipt, `EEXIST` preserved both collision files,
and native-off failed closed. An injected post-rename directory-sync failure
preserved the complete final file and returned a `targetCreated: true` failure
receipt. The package offers no public synchronous no-replace publication API.
XMODEM now has an async `publishing` state: EOT ACK/completion waits for the
publisher, repeated EOT/cancel/start cannot interrupt the commit, and
destroy/restart generation fencing prevents stale completion or staging cleanup
from mutating a new session. The focused protocol/local-PTY/SSH-PTY suites pass
22/22; [the lifecycle record](evidence/IR03-XMODEM-ASYNC-PUBLICATION-2026-09-24.md)
documents the behavior and commit-boundary limitation. The
[native candidate record](evidence/IR03-ATOMIC-PUBLICATION-NATIVE-POC-2026-09-24.md)
captures the exact provider semantics. This lifecycle record predates the
native worktree trial; rights review, real filesystem and three-platform
evidence remain. IR-03 is **In progress**.

The same exact native candidate now has a verified npm publication-origin

The [official npm provenance chain](evidence/IR11-FS-SAFE-NPM-ATTESTED-PROVENANCE-2026-09-24.md)

The [macOS fs-safe Cargo source-scope SPDX record](evidence/IR11-FS-SAFE-MACOS-SOURCE-SBOM-2026-09-24.md)

The [nested Zstandard legal-text record](evidence/IR11-ZSTD-NESTED-LEGAL-2026-09-24.md)

The P-08 release-owner handoff and packaging guide were refreshed against the

documentation regression links the handoff count to the core review ledger and

A fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory package was built to an isolated

P-08/IR-13's local signed updater now accepts a chunked artifact response
without `Content-Length`, while still requiring the signed manifest's exact
streamed size and SHA-256. It loops over short filesystem writes instead of
marking a truncated installer ready, rejects zero-progress writes, and reports
download-directory setup failures through its stable status boundary. Both
new cases failed before correction; the focused suite now passes 11/11. A
fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory app matched its SPDX sidecar and passed
the packaged updater journey 1/1 using a local Ed25519-signed, chunked HTTP
fixture. Exact hashes and limits are in the
[updater stream-write record](evidence/IR13-UPDATER-STREAM-WRITES-2026-09-24.md).
This is not a controlled production HTTPS feed, signed installer or installed
upgrade; IR-13 remains **Open**.

P-08/IR-13's updater version boundary now follows SemVer 2.0.0 instead of
treating all prereleases with the same numeric base as equal. New signed-feed
regressions first failed for `alpha` → `alpha.1`, `beta.2` → `beta.11` and
numeric → alphanumeric prerelease progression; the Contract also accepted
malformed prerelease identifiers. The corrected Contract bounds and validates
full SemVer including build metadata, while the Host compares prerelease
identifiers correctly and ignores build metadata for precedence. The focused
updater suite passes 19/19, typecheck and 267-operation Contract check pass,
and a fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory app matches its SPDX sidecar and
passes the packaged signed-updater journey 1/1. Exact hashes and limitations
are in the [SemVer updater record](evidence/IR13-UPDATER-SEMVER-PRECEDENCE-2026-09-24.md).
No production feed, signing identity or real prerelease-package upgrade is
claimed; IR-13 remains **Open**.

P-08/IR-13 next closed an unsigned-field gap in the signed update manifest:
`notes` was present in the strict JSON but absent from the Ed25519 canonical
record. It is now signed as a JSON-escaped seventh field, so changing only the
served release notes yields `UPDATE_SIGNATURE_INVALID`. Both production and
packaged Electron fixtures use the shared canonical function, avoiding
six-field test copies. The focused updater suite passes 20/20; typecheck,
build, the production Electron H12 journey and a fresh unsigned macOS arm64
packaged updater journey each pass. Packaged Resources match their SPDX
sidecar; exact hashes and limits are in the [manifest-note signature
record](evidence/IR13-UPDATER-NOTES-SIGNATURE-2026-09-24.md). The public signer
must use the new seven-field format, but no production feed exists to migrate.
This does not establish signed/notarized installers or real cross-platform
upgrades; IR-13 remains **Open**.

P-08/IR-13 now has a source-controlled, non-secret `UPDATE_FEED_RECORD.json`
for a public manifest URL, Ed25519 public key and release-owner approval.
Packaged applications read a copy from their Resources; an `active` package
pins those values ahead of process environment, while a missing/malformed
copy reports `UPDATE_CONFIGURATION_INVALID`. The current record is honestly
`pending`: `release:updater-feed:check` passes, but the new
`release:updater-feed:active-check` fails and is a prerequisite of
`release:final-public:check`. Three installed-package gates and packaging CI
now compare package bytes to the root record. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64
directory app contained the exact pending record, passed the package-byte
check, matched its SPDX sidecar and passed the packaged signed-updater journey
1/1 with an explicit loopback fixture. Focused tests passed 31/31; hashes and
limits are in the [packaged update-feed record](evidence/IR13-PACKAGED-UPDATE-FEED-2026-09-24.md).
This is not a configured public feed or Windows/native-Linux installed result;
IR-13 remains **Open**.

The same P-08/IR-13 source then produced a new unsigned macOS arm64 DMG and

matched its packaged SPDX sidecar and the source `UPDATE_FEED_RECORD.json`
byte-for-byte; its full packaged suite passed **21 applicable journeys** with

P-03/IR-04 now rejects a deterministic outside-root ancestor-symlink swap
during FTP read-handle opening. `SIZE`, `MDTM` and `RETR` compare the resolved
path and pre-/post-open file identities with the opened handle before returning
metadata or bytes, and close rejected handles. The real-FTP Adapter suite
passed 30/30; the Linux arm64/x64 source smokes now run the same injected swap
and pass; and a fresh unsigned macOS
arm64 directory package matched its SPDX sidecar and passed the packaged FTP
Widget journey 1/1. The exact scope and hashes are in the [IR-04 read-handle
record](evidence/IR04-FTP-READ-HANDLE-IDENTITY-2026-09-24.md). Node's path-based
checks are not an atomic confinement primitive; independent security review
and Windows/native-Linux installed evidence remain open. IR-04 stays **In
progress** and the accepted count remains 2/14.

Linux x64 installed evidence, but remains **emulated**, unsigned and without a
native Linux desktop, external SSH or historical-binary upgrade.
IR-04 stays **In progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

The same audited source snapshot now also produced a Linux x64 AppImage with

The Linux CI workflow now has a separate `test:appimage:linux:runtime` gate
after the static payload check, both before upload and after the downloaded
artifact's hash/SPDX checks. It launches a temporary AppImage copy under Xvfb
with extract-and-run, binds the copied bytes to the release-hash manifest before

version, Desktop Runtime readiness and local terminal tabs, and retains a SHA-256-linked runtime receipt
from the downloaded run. This worktree's gate wiring and Playwright discovery
are checked locally; `bun run check` passes 222 test files (1,124 passed,
33 skipped), 359 architecture modules / 1,310 dependencies, all 267 Contract
operations, build/package layout and 11 visual/accessibility journeys. No
successful native Linux x64 CI result or receipt exists yet; this is **not**
a change to the IR-13 Open state. The exact scope
and remaining proof are in the [AppImage runtime-gate record](evidence/IR13-LINUX-APPIMAGE-RUNTIME-GATE-2026-09-24.md).

P-06's legacy remote backup now has a byte-oriented `SyncProvider.readBackup`
boundary separate from normal sync decoding. WebDAV and raw Custom responses
retain their exact bytes, including malformed UTF-8; normal Custom sync loading
now rejects invalid UTF-8 instead of replacing bytes silently. Gist/Gitee and
named-file Custom APIs expose file content as JSON strings, so their backup is
explicitly documented as the UTF-8 encoding of the provider-returned field.
The 24 MiB bound, save-grant atomic write, SHA-256, read-only behavior and
provider credential boundary remain. Focused provider/Application tests cover
opaque WebDAV and Custom bytes, byte count/hash, strict normal-load rejection
and oversize response refusal. A fresh isolated unsigned macOS arm64 directory
app then passed the packaged legacy-WebDAV migration/recovery and
GitHub/Gitee/named-file-Custom journeys (2/2; `app.asar` SHA-256
`f2b1c15017e32b0cf98a3751966541a10df459435ee99b2627b8f0325b591eee`). The
final `bun run check` passes 220 test files (1,085 passed, 33 skipped), 358
architecture modules / 1,307 dependencies, 267 Contract operations, the
independent snapshot/build/package-layout gates and all 11 visual/accessibility
journeys. This does not change IR-08 acceptance: real provider accounts, public migration
release/window and Windows/native-Linux installed paths remain outstanding. See
the updated [IR-08 remote-backup evidence](evidence/IR08-REMOTE-BACKUP-2026-09-20.md).

The current P-06 follow-up also handles GitHub Gist files marked `truncated`:
load and backup fetch the validated official raw URL without forwarding the
Gist API credential or following redirects, then retain the 24 MiB response
bound. Backup preserves raw response bytes; ordinary sync rejects malformed
UTF-8. A truncated PATCH response returns the just-uploaded content instead
of reporting an error after the remote write. Gitee truncation remains
fail-closed, and GitHub's documented over-10-MiB clone path is not implemented.
Focused adapter and Application-boundary coverage and the remaining provider/platform limits are in the
[IR-08 remote-backup evidence](evidence/IR08-REMOTE-BACKUP-2026-09-20.md).
After this follow-up and its documentation edits, `bun run check` passed again:
220 test files passed, 5 skipped; 1,090 tests passed, 33 skipped; 358
architecture modules / 1,307 dependencies; all 267 Contract operations; build,
package-layout, source-snapshot and all 11 visual/accessibility journeys.

P-06 now checks GitHub's declared byte size against the validated raw-file

267 Contract operations, the license/SBOM/source-snapshot and production
package-layout gates, plus all 11 visual/accessibility journeys. The core

P-03's current-reuse ledger had retained wording from the removed implementation,
incorrectly claiming the same MIT `@legacy-prototype/ftp-srv` engine and naming the
deleted `legacy-prototype-local-ftp-server.ts` Adapter. `UPSTREAM.md` now describes the
Axterm-authored Node TCP/filesystem server, identifies `basic-ftp` as a test
client only, and keeps third-party source/provenance review separate. The
independent-release documentation test locks that distinction. This corrects
release-facing provenance documentation; it does not satisfy IR-02/IR-04's
qualified review or Windows/native-Linux package evidence.

P-03 now also implements the FTP default ASCII representation as streaming
NVT-ASCII translation: `RETR` emits network CRLF/CR-NUL, `STOR` maps network
CRLF to the host newline convention, `SIZE` counts transfer octets, and `REST`
resumes `RETR`/`STOR` at transfer-octet offsets, including offsets inside those
two-byte sequences. The focused FTP adapter suite passes 25 tests; its ASCII
regression resumes STOR at every octet boundary of the fixture stream, then
re-downloads and compares the exact NVT bytes, including boundaries inside
CRLF and CR-NUL. It also checks ASCII `SIZE`, `TYPE I` and host newline
conversion.
`bun run check` passes on 2026-09-24: 221 test files (1,096 passed, 33 skipped),
359 architecture modules / 1,310 dependencies, all 267 Contract operations,
independent-snapshot, licensing, SBOM, production build/package-layout and all
11 visual/accessibility journeys. This advances local IR-04 implementation
evidence only; independent protocol/security review and Windows/native-Linux
packaged evidence remain required.

The current FTP source-runtime smoke now also covers ASCII conversion and
restart on Linux containers. The pinned Bun image passed both `linux/arm64` and
`linux/amd64` runs, including binary login/list/transfer, denial, passive-socket
lifecycle, NVT `SIZE`/`RETR`, Linux newline conversion and `REST STOR` offsets
inside CRLF and CR-NUL. Exact commands, image digest, source hashes and limits
are in the [IR-04 Linux FTP container record](evidence/IR04-FTP-LINUX-CONTAINER-2026-09-24.md).
These are container source-runtime checks, not native Linux or packaged-app
evidence, so IR-04 remains **In progress**.

The current FTP implementation also passes the packaged macOS arm64 FTP Widget
journey outside the checkout (1/1). The run validates actual package Resources
against a generated SPDX sidecar and exercises binary plus curl ASCII
upload/download, active/passive data connections, rename and shutdown. Exact

record](evidence/IR04-PACKAGED-MACOS-FTP-ASCII-2026-09-24.md). This is unsigned
macOS directory-package evidence only; Windows/native-Linux installed proof
and independent protocol/security review remain outstanding, so IR-04 remains
**In progress**.

The preceding FTP ASCII implementation was rebuilt from an audited 817-file
independent snapshot into Linux x64 DEB and AppImage artifacts. A disposable

packaged journeys, including the FTP binary/ASCII Widget path; eight declared

record](evidence/IR13-LINUX-X64-FTP-ASCII-PACKAGED-2026-09-24.md) contains
source/artifact hashes and the test-host prerequisites. This is emulated Linux
evidence, not native Linux, Windows, signing or independent security/rights
review; IR-04 stays **In progress** and IR-13 stays **Open**.

A follow-up P-03 review found an actual leading-newline `REST STOR` failure:
`REST 1` maps to zero local prefix bytes, but the server attempted a read stream
ending at `-1` and returned 550. The upload now skips the empty prefix copy
and exclusively creates the staging file for the resumed network bytes. The
expanded all-offset unit fixture passes 25/25, and real FTP source-runtime
smokes pass on Linux arm64 and x64 at offsets 1, 4 and 7. A fresh macOS arm64
directory package passes its FTP journey (1/1). A new audited 818-file source
snapshot produced Linux x64 DEB/AppImage artifacts; the installed DEB passes
all 23 applicable packaged journeys with eight declared skips, while the
extracted AppImage legal payload, installed Resources, SPDX sidecar and release
hashes all agree. Exact hashes, the before/after failure and platform limits
are in the [leading REST evidence](evidence/IR04-FTP-ASCII-LEADING-REST-2026-09-24.md).
This strengthens IR-04/IR-13 engineering evidence but leaves independent
security/source-rights review, Windows and native-Linux release gates open;
IR-04 remains **In progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

A further P-03 File Grant review found that destructive FTP commands targeting
an in-root symbolic link could mutate the referenced file or empty directory:
the new regression observed `DELE file-link` returning `250` and deleting its
target before the fix. `DELE`, `RMD` and `RNFR` now reject final-component
symlinks after confining the parent. The real FTP Adapter suite passes 26/26,
and the Linux arm64/x64 source-runtime smokes pass the new three-command
denial check. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory package matched its SPDX
sidecar and passed the extended packaged FTP journey 1/1. The exact before/after
behavior, source/package hashes and the residual path-operation race are in
the [IR-04 symlink mutation evidence](evidence/IR04-FTP-SYMLINK-MUTATION-2026-09-24.md).
An audited independent snapshot also produced updated Linux x64 DEB and
AppImage candidates. The installed DEB passed 23 applicable packaged journeys
with eight declared skips on the final run, including the new FTP guard; the
AppImage and installed Resources matched the SPDX sidecar, and copied release
hashes verified. Two preceding full DEB attempts exposed an intermittent
emulated-host focus failure in the migration accessibility journey; the test
now brings the window forward before each keyboard activation, retains its
focus assertion and passed separately and in the final full run. This is
emulated Linux, not native-Linux sign-off. Independent security/source-rights
review and Windows/native-Linux installed evidence remain open; IR-04 stays
**In progress** and IR-13 **Open**.
The current worktree's complete `bun run check` passed after the fix and test
updates: 221 test files passed / 5 skipped; 1,097 tests passed / 33 skipped;
359 architecture modules / 1,310 dependencies; 267 Contract operations;
source snapshot, license/SBOM, build/package layout and all 11 Axterm visual
and accessibility journeys. This is engineering verification, not a release
approval or independent rights review.

P-03/IR-04 next closed a directory-listing resource-bound gap: the FTP server
previously called `readdir` before applying its 1,000-entry limit, so an
authorized directory could be materialized without a fixed enumeration bound.
`LIST`, `NLST` and `MLSD` now iterate through `opendir` with a 32-entry
internal buffer and reject the 1,001st entry. The real FTP Adapter suite
passes 27/27; it checks each verb's 550 response, continued control-connection
use, and success at exactly 1,000 entries. Linux arm64/x64 source-runtime
smokes and a freshly built unsigned macOS arm64 packaged FTP journey also pass.
Exact commands, source/package hashes and evidence limits are in the
[bounded-list record](evidence/IR04-FTP-BOUNDED-LIST-2026-09-24.md). The current
package is not signed or installed; independent security/source-rights review
and Windows/native-Linux installed evidence remain open. IR-04 stays **In
progress** and IR-13 **Open**. The full `bun run check` passed after this change:
221 test files passed / 5 skipped, 1,098 tests passed / 33 skipped, and all 11
visual/accessibility journeys passed.

The same IR-04 bounded-list change now has a new Linux x64 DEB candidate built
from an audited 821-file independent source snapshot under emulation. The
installed Resources matched its packaged SPDX sidecar, and all 23 applicable
packaged journeys passed, including the 1,001-entry FTP rejection and the GTK
launcher icon check; eight Windows-only, historical-binary or opt-in SSH
journeys skipped. The Linux installer gate now forwards the exact DEB path to
the icon journey instead of silently skipping it when an isolated artifact
directory is used, and checks its Python GI/GTK prerequisite up front. The
unsigned DEB, sidecar and release-hash manifest are retained and verified in

record](evidence/IR04-FTP-BOUNDED-LIST-2026-09-24.md) has exact hashes,
commands, the intermediate failed attempts and limits. This is an installed
emulated-Linux result, not native-Linux or Windows sign-off; IR-04 remains **In
progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

P-03/IR-04 also closes a resumed-upload descriptor leak: `REST STOR` opened
its original file before validating `stat` and the TYPE A restart position,
but exceptions from either operation bypassed the close path. A real FTP
control-session regression injected `EIO` into both operations; the read case
failed before the fix with zero closes, and after the fix both cases return
`550`, close the handle exactly once, preserve the original bytes and accept
another command. The focused Adapter file passes 29/29. A fresh unsigned
macOS arm64 directory package matched its SPDX sidecar and passed the packaged
FTP Widget journey 1/1. Exact source/package hashes and limits are in the
[restart-handle cleanup record](evidence/IR04-FTP-RESTART-HANDLE-CLEANUP-2026-09-24.md).
The same corrected source was then rebuilt from an audited 822-file
independent snapshot into a new Linux x64 DEB. A disposable emulated-x64
container installed it, checked actual Resources against the packaged SPDX
sidecar, and passed all 23 applicable packaged journeys with eight declared
skips, including FTP, migration accessibility and the GTK launcher icon.
The unsigned DEB, sidecar and verified release-hash manifest are retained in
ignored `release/axterm-linux-ftp-restart-20260924/`; exact source/artifact
hashes and limits are in the same evidence record. This advances lifecycle
and installed-package engineering evidence only. Independent security and
source-rights review, Windows/native-Linux installed evidence, signing and
upgrades remain open; IR-04 stays **In progress** and IR-13 **Open**.

P-06's independent Axterm sync preview now applies its selected
`addressBookmarks` and `workspaces` categories after the broad `settings`
category, because those collections are represented in both places. A
regression commits the same remote document with forward and reverse category
orders and verifies both dedicated collections remain restored in SQLite. This
prevents selection order from silently discarding migrated user data; IR-08
remains In progress because the public release, cross-platform and historical
artifact gates remain open.

P-07's rendered accessibility gate now runs axe-core 4.13.0 as a test-only
dependency inside the source Electron renderer without changing production
CSP. It scans the startup shell, Settings common/legal/sync workspaces
(including expanded product-license and third-party-notice documents), Host
Manager with imported records, Add SSH and RDP dialogs, terminal-theme
workspace, Widgets, the actual Legacy Prototype-data import preview and success/failure
states, and the independent Axterm configuration preview for
WCAG 2.0 A/AA, 2.1 A/AA, 2.2 AA and axe best-practice findings, alongside the
existing keyboard/name/focus and reduced-motion checks. The rendered audit
found and fixed a mislabeled host group filter (`tablist` changed to a named
toggle group with `aria-pressed`), multiple low-contrast hint/metric/primary
text colors, incorrect heading levels, invalid Widget listbox semantics during
loading, nested `main` landmarks, missing visible headings in the Theme and
Widget workspaces, and license text scroll regions that were not keyboard
focusable when expanded. License and notice bodies now receive focus from their
disclosure summaries and show a visible focus ring. Adding the actual
Legacy Prototype-data preview revealed an invalid table/row ARIA structure: migration
entries are now a named native list, and action filters expose their selected
state with `aria-pressed`. The keyboard E2E opens a synthetic import through the
native File Grant, scans the rendered preview, changes filters, then cancels.
An invalid JSON retry exposed that migration failures and blocked independent
configuration previews were not announced as alerts; both now use `role=alert`,
and the E2E selects malformed JSON to verify the rendered failure state.
Auditing the completed migration and opening the imported Host Manager also
exposed low contrast on its 9 px connection-state copy (3.95:1); it now uses
Axterm's semantic muted-text token, and both the imported Host Manager and
independent configuration preview remain in the keyboard journey. The focused
accessibility project passes 2/2 journeys across fourteen axe-audited rendered
scenes. The latest full `bun run check` passes 219 test files (1,028 passed,
33 skipped), nine visual and two accessibility journeys, plus architecture,
Contract, release-hygiene and build gates. A separate unsigned macOS arm64
directory package also passes one targeted keyboard-activated
migration/accessibility journey across six rendered states; its exact artifact
hash and limits are recorded in
[IR-10 packaged accessibility evidence](evidence/IR10-PACKAGED-A11Y-2026-09-23.md).
This remains engineering evidence—not manual assistive-technology review,
exhaustive surface coverage, Windows/native-Linux evidence or human design
sign-off; IR-10 remains **In progress**. See the
[Axterm visual and accessibility baseline](AXTERM_VISUAL_BASELINE.md).

`ec3f66a43fe038122afc4e4a06d2c198847802a64f634f0a19b6d65b266e01b7` and the

evidence; its follow-up is recorded below.

A second `bun run candidate:protocol:check` on 2026-09-24 passed the named

The newest `candidate:protocol:check` replay again passed from a clean

P-06 also hardens the native installer checks for migration-period links. The
Linux DEB gate now requires the installed desktop entry to list both
`x-scheme-handler/axterm` and `x-scheme-handler/legacy-prototype`. The Windows NSIS
gate refuses to run when either protocol already has a registration, then
checks the installed app's URL Protocol marker and open command; cleanup
removes only registrations that point to its exact temporary install. The
macOS DMG gate now checks the copied installed bundle's `Info.plist` declares
both URL schemes. The existing three-platform CI invokes these installer
gates. Focused transition-boundary tests and the full scan pass with all three
scripts explicitly enumerated; the Linux shell parses and its MIME predicate
passed a focused sample. A subsequent clean non-local candidate clone also
passed the full source checks, visual/accessibility journeys, production
Electron E2E and pinned SSH protocol peers with these scripts present. This is
installer-test hardening, not Linux/Windows release evidence; no native Linux
installation was run, and PowerShell is not installed here, so the Linux and
Windows script bodies have not been executed on their target operating systems.
IR-08 and IR-13 remain unaccepted. See the [Linux installer gate](../../scripts/test-linux-deb.sh),
[macOS DMG gate](../../scripts/test-macos-dmg.sh), and
[Windows installer gate](../../scripts/test-windows-nsis.ps1), plus the
[latest IR-12 candidate record](evidence/IR12-EPHEMERAL-GIT-CANDIDATE-2026-09-21.md).

P-07 packaged macOS testing exposed that the overlaid right-side toolbar could
sit above the first terminal pane's Add Terminal button; the toolbar's
Ellipsis SVG intercepted pointer clicks. Terminal pane tab bars now reserve
the measured overlay width on every platform, and the Axterm visual journey
asserts that the Add Terminal button itself receives a center-point hit test.
Two packaged journeys also retained stale `tab`/`option` role locators after
those Settings and Widget controls became ordinary buttons; the tests now use
their actual button semantics. A targeted source visual check passed, and the
H-11 source-Electron language journey passed. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64
package generated into an isolated output directory passed the full DMG gate:
the image verifies and mounts, the copied app's scheme declaration and SPDX
sidecar match, the image detaches before launch, and 20 packaged journeys pass
with ten Linux/Windows/optional-fixture skips. Its `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`0f53c1a1bb80b8a6650ceb34bc8221639b4e188e025a4c79f2f611801221fe45`, its DMG
SHA-256 is `4af14cde7edbff6515bc9b0adfc66b63593e591412c858f87d6267b142599c4b`,
and its SPDX sidecar SHA-256 is
`cc4d1b1ad2e3fbc973acc48ef6848c1fe7b0771bc837c2c752c7b5a5db2a2db0`. An
existing local `release/` app/DMG and sidecar were also checked and did not
match, so those stale outputs are not release evidence and must not be shipped;
they were left untouched. This is unsigned local macOS engineering evidence,
not signing/notarization, update-feed verification, manual design review or
Windows/Linux proof; IR-10 and IR-13 remain **In progress/Open** respectively.

P-05/P-07 now also prevent a stale platform icon from reintroducing the retired
blue oval mark after the SVG redesign. `bun run icons:check` renders PNG, ICO and
ICNS variants from the mint-green AX source into an isolated temporary directory
and requires byte equality without modifying checked-in assets. Its
macOS-only integration test is included in `bun run check`; the focused icon
and shell-brand tests pass 4/4, including the existing assertion that terminal
tab numbers use the green primary token. This verifies source-to-variant
consistency, not installed Windows/Linux appearance or trademark clearance;
IR-07/IR-10 remain **In progress**. The latest `bun run check` on 2026-09-24
passed 220 test files with five skipped (1,080 passed, 33 skipped), 358
architecture modules / 1,307 dependencies, all 267 Contract operations, all
commercialization and source-snapshot checks, production build/package layout,
nine visual journeys and two accessibility journeys. Its source snapshot
contains 811 files / 18,349,521 bytes with SHA-256
`a5a11f5ca340e9d1f177190f0d01c5737bd1992717baad1bff29ac837016e5eb`. These are
local source and macOS engineering gates; they do not replace pending
rights/language/brand review, signed distribution or native Windows/Linux
installed-app evidence.

P-06/IR-08 now converts Legacy Prototype's documented SSH, Local, Telnet, Serial, RDP,
VNC, FTP, SPICE and Web bookmark shapes. Protocol credentials and authenticated
proxy passwords go through the application-local Vault; Connection Profile
references are remapped, and legacy `runScripts` are omitted without running.
SQLite migration 39 widens import identity kinds while preserving existing SSH
mappings and producing a pre-migration backup. Synthetic coverage verifies all
eight non-SSH protocols, repeat-import idempotency, group/startup associations
and secret redaction. The preview also reports no mapped fields for skipped
records. A separate sanitized derivative from Legacy Prototype v1.101.16's official
Linux arm64 UI export adds seven genuine non-SSH record shapes to the
version-controlled fixture; six convert, while the blank-username RDP record is
explicitly skipped. That export exposed a preview/commit mismatch, now fixed so
the unsupported record is disclosed before commit. The focused importer suite
passes 15/15. SPICE remains covered by a documented-shape synthetic fixture;
the released UI did not yield a SPICE export. IR-08 remains **In progress**
because public migration-release/window, installer and cross-platform evidence
are still missing; see [P-06 coverage](P06_MIGRATION_COVERAGE.md) and the
privately archived UI-export evidence.

P-06 theme migration now also previews and transactionally imports valid saved
Legacy Prototype `terminalThemes` rows, maps each source ID to its new user-theme ID,
and remaps a selected custom theme in the same import. Incomplete palettes use
Axterm Default values for missing fields; malformed rows are disclosed as
skipped, unavailable legacy selections fall back to Axterm Default, and preview
does not expose palette values. A regression confirms that selecting an
imported theme preserves the user's existing Axterm terminal background.
Ambiguous duplicate theme IDs and rows beyond the 256-theme bound are skipped
with no mapped fields. SQLite migration 40 widens the import-mapping kind
constraint without discarding mappings created by migration 39. Runtime and
desktop journeys now consume a fixed-hash sanitized derivative of a custom
theme created and exported through the official Legacy Prototype v1.101.16 UI. They
verify palette preservation, redacted preview, selected-theme remapping and
repeat-import idempotency. Automatic `default` / `defaultLight` rows are skipped
without importing upstream palettes; selected built-ins map to Axterm's own
Default / Default light. The migration limit counts only user themes, even when
the export includes the two automatic built-ins. The fixture retains the
fictional test theme and selected ID plus only the built-ins' IDs and names.
The focused importer/database/data-sync suite passes 49/49; `bun run build` passes, and
the exact native-file-grant desktop E2E passes 1/1. A separate copied-outside-
checkout macOS arm64 package journey also passes: the field-redacted preview
skips both built-in rows, imports the custom palette, remaps selection, and
persists exactly one target user-theme row. A repeat packaged import reports
the theme and selected setting as unchanged, disables the zero-item commit,
and still leaves one database row. The full packaged suite passes 21/31 with
ten explicit platform/fixture skips; exact hashes and limitations
are recorded in [packaged macOS suite evidence](evidence/PACKAGED-MACOS-ARM64-SUITE-2026-09-24.md).
These are migration regressions using a sanitized derivative of a fictional
custom theme created and exported through the official Legacy Prototype v1.101.16 UI;
they are not a public migration release or source-rights approval. IR-08/IR-06
remain **In progress**.

P-07/IR-13 current Linux x64 packaged validation on 2026-09-24 exposed and
fixed a low-contrast transition: migration preview primary buttons animated
foreground and background together while switching from disabled to enabled,
and Linux axe sampled intermediate 2.76:1–3.47:1 colors. Primary actions now
transition only their border color, with a source regression test; the packaged
keyboard journey explicitly foregrounds the application before focus checks.
A fresh 810-file clean snapshot (tree SHA-256
`b2ba44caa8bbcd2c053d73d60c82897577924518cfd4dac6b84137218f9fb599`) built the
Linux x64 0.10.0 DEB and AppImage in isolated output. Frozen install, production
build, packaging layout, SPDX generation/check, release hashes/check and the
installed DEB gate passed. The installed `/opt/Axterm` resource tree matched
the SPDX sidecar exactly; 22 packaged journeys passed and 9 declared
platform/historical/optional-fixture journeys skipped, with no failures. The
formerly failing migration/configuration accessibility and custom-theme
migration journeys passed. Static AppImage payload verification also matched
the sidecar and ASAR hash. Exact artifact hashes and emulation limits are in
[IR-13 Linux contrast evidence](evidence/IR13-LINUX-X64-PACKAGED-CONTRAST-2026-09-24.md).
This is emulated Debian `linux/amd64` engineering evidence—not native Linux,
signing, public upgrade or release-owner approval—so IR-10/IR-13 remain open.

P-08/IR-13 current macOS arm64 installation was revalidated on 2026-09-24
from the current working tree. A fresh unsigned `0.10.0` DMG and ZIP were
built to an isolated temporary directory, leaving repository `release/`
untouched. The DMG CRC and ZIP contents passed; the read-only mounted app was
copied to a separate install directory, the DMG detached before launch, and
the installed Resources matched the generated SPDX 2.3 sidecar exactly. All
21 macOS-applicable packaged journeys passed and 10 Windows/Linux,
historical-public-installer or optional SSH-fixture journeys skipped. This
includes the current migration, locale, license/About, transfer, Shell and
prior-schema/Vault package journeys. Artifact and ASAR hashes, exact procedure

It is unsigned and does not establish notarization, public updater or
historical-user upgrade, native Windows/Linux, migration-window, legal/design

The DMG test script now uses macOS `diskutil image attach` with explicit
read-only/mount-point options instead of the deprecated `hdiutil attach`; a
second full DMG gate passed using that path without the deprecation warning.

The migration guide now names the exact legacy bookmark shapes recognized by
the importer and distinguishes the synthetic non-SSH fixture scope from
published-binary evidence. It also corrects the credential instructions:
credential-metadata rows alone cannot restore a Vault secret, while supported
inline values present in the selected file are written directly to the local
Vault, never previewed or stored in product SQLite. The guide now tells users
to treat source files as sensitive and verify imported connections instead of
assuming either that all passwords transfer or that none can.

P-06 now additionally has a formatting-normalized fixture generated by the
public `legacy-prototype-data-tool` 2.1.10 NeDB-v1 exporter from synthetic records.
This exposed and fixed an `_id` identity gap affecting bookmark/group/Profile/
Quick Command IDs and their references. A regression imports four protocols,
the hierarchy, Profile, command and startup selection, while confirming that
secrets remain out of preview and SQLite. The focused importer suite passes
14/14. These are exporter-shaped synthetic records, not an Legacy Prototype desktop
binary or real-user export; IR-08 remains **In progress**. See the
[P-06 migration coverage](P06_MIGRATION_COVERAGE.md) and committed fixture.

A safety attempt to run the signed Legacy Prototype v1.101.16 release with a temporary
`HOME` confirmed that macOS Electron still used the current account's appData.
It saved only a synthetic test Bookmark; no export or credentials were used.
The ten newly created NeDB files and new Electron Preferences file were moved
recoverably to Trash. Existing SQLite and Vault-key file modification times
were unchanged, but cache/session metadata was touched, so we cannot claim the
whole app profile was untouched. No further production-binary run against the
host macOS profile is safe without a separate macOS account or OS-level
filesystem isolation. IR-08 remains **In progress**; details and limits are
recorded in [P-06 coverage](P06_MIGRATION_COVERAGE.md).

A separate safe run installed the official Legacy Prototype v1.101.16 Linux arm64
release inside an isolated Debian container whose only host mount was a unique

data file; a secret-free derivative preserves seven UI-created non-SSH
bookmarks. The focused importer test verifies six conversions and the explicit
skip reason for an RDP record whose required username was absent. This
containerized result does not make further runs against the host macOS app
profile safe, nor does it replace source-rights review or Windows/native-Linux
migration evidence. Exact asset/export/fixture hashes and isolation limits are
in the private IR-08 UI-export evidence archive.

P-07/IR-10 now has a four-locale Settings layout regression for English,
Japanese, Simplified Chinese and Traditional Chinese. Review found that the
Japanese and Simplified Chinese baseline captures had left the Language card
below the viewport even though a small part of its region intersected it. The
test now centers the actual card and requires both the whole card and selector
to fit vertically, while continuing to check horizontal overflow. All four
1280×800 macOS captures were regenerated and visually inspected with the
Language card in view. The card's remaining hard-coded `LANGUAGE` eyebrow was
replaced with Axterm's existing translated Settings label; source visual tests
assert the exact English, Japanese and Chinese strings at normal and 200% zoom.
A companion 200% UI-zoom journey switches through all four locales in the
720×450 CSS viewport and requires the full card and selector to remain within
bounds without horizontal overflow. Both focused journeys pass. This is
source-Electron layout evidence, not translation quality, design sign-off or
Windows/Linux baseline evidence; IR-10 remains **In progress**. See the
[Axterm visual baseline](AXTERM_VISUAL_BASELINE.md).

P-04/P-07 now also have a current-worktree macOS arm64 **directory-package**

P-04 also now has a focused macOS arm64 packaged-upgrade regression for a

On 2026-09-24, a fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory package passed two focused

P-04/P-06 now checks language migration against the full former 15-entry
Legacy Prototype locale list and each recorded `upstreamId` alias from historical
commit `657b3cc`. The four retained locales map to `en`, `ja`, `zh-CN` and

preview reason, while unknown values remain reported as omitted. SQLite

remains **In progress**.

P-07's final local axe rerun exposed a transient disabled-control contrast

and cross-platform evidence are still missing.

unsigned macOS arm64 directory app passes 17 packaged journeys, with ten

the DEB SHA-256 is

P-06 closed a self-export size mismatch in the migration-period data-file
path: it could write a valid file up to 16 MiB but refused to preview anything
over 8 MiB. Import and export now share one 16 MiB bound, and the importer
checks both the authorized file and its opened descriptor before allocation.
A regression creates 60 real Quick Commands, exports a file between 8 and
16 MiB, imports and commits it into a fresh database, verifies all commands
and steps, and rejects a file one byte over 16 MiB. The focused nine-test
suite and complete `bun run check` pass. This removes one recoverability gap;
it does not start the public migration clock or accept IR-08.

The independent `axterm-configuration` v1 path now has a matching large-file
round-trip regression: 60 real Quick Commands produce an 8–16 MiB export,
which a fresh database previews and commits with all 60 commands and eight
steps per command intact. This verifies the final independent portable format
at the same practical size range as the migration-period compatibility file;
it is not a complete Vault backup or three-platform package acceptance.

P-06's release-record removal gate no longer counts the initial public

later version and a public date after the first migration release; the focused
validator suite covers these cases. This prevents a premature
`migration:record:removal-check` pass from record duplication alone. The actual
record remains `pending`; no public migration clock or IR-09 removal approval
is inferred. See the [migration coverage audit](P06_MIGRATION_COVERAGE.md).

terminal-transfer protocols, exact license/About material, RDP initialization,
migration warning/conversion, signed-updater boundary, checkout-independent
startup, four-language switching/restart and prior-schema/Vault upgrade. The
tested `app.asar` is 101,524,687 bytes with SHA-256
`a0ee04c2edca7712798efb09c36b14ba571d4b4799308c321bc47cd89b5eae70`; the
packaged `icon.icns` SHA-256 is
`cdeedd28c8e4171e9111d44eef618c2aa7879568cae8e987514321005759d205`.
This closes the current macOS directory-app color/mark evidence gap, not signed
installation, Windows/Linux packaged design review, trademark approval or
IR-07/IR-10 acceptance.

**P-02 selected external-peer silence and malformed-frame cleanup — verified
with fixed package bytes.**
On 2026-09-22, the opt-in `external-lrzsz-ssh-pty` regression added the
selected-XMODEM failure path: after an actual Debian `sx` peer receives Axterm's
CRC request, the test deliberately withholds later peer frames from the product
engine, advances the bounded retry clock and requires `session-error`,
`session-end`, no published file, a nonzero peer exit and labelled-container
cleanup. It also first confirms a real selected-destination `.part` file, then
corrupts later external `sx` frames in transit while the SSH channel and peer
remain open; the bounded retry limit must cancel the peer and remove staging.
The disposable Docker APT-index path exceeded its fixed 40-second hook limit on
this host, so the test now also accepts an explicitly supplied temporary
`AXTERM_EXTERNAL_LRZSZ_DEB` path. It copies that input only into its own
temporary fixture and requires the existing fixed Debian SHA-256 before the
external peer is installed in a network-isolated container. Directly acquired
arm64 and amd64 package bytes matched their expected hashes; for each selected
architecture the direct-PTY suite passed 6/6 and the real encrypted SSH-PTY
suite passed 16/16. In addition to selected-XMODEM silence, it now proves that
both actual `sx` and actual `sz` peers have first written nonzero selected
staging bytes before a later in-transit payload fault forces cancellation and
cleanup. The independently hash-pinned `trzsz-go` SSH-PTY suite also passed
7/7 on arm64 and amd64: after nonzero staging bytes, it corrupts a later
encoded `#DATA:` payload character while retaining the peer/channel, then
requires Axterm's cancellation, `session-error`, `session-end`, no staging and
no publication. No peer package entered the repository, lockfile, production
dependency graph or release payload. The ordinary no-Docker XMODEM, ZMODEM and
TRZSZ protocol suites separately verify their cancellation semantics. This is
valid controlled Debian/trzsz-go peer evidence, but not independently operated
SSH, installed macOS/Windows/Linux, native-bad-frame behavior in upstream
binaries, arbitrary non-channel-close partial-file failure coverage,
source-rights or IR-03 acceptance.

On 2026-09-22, ADR-018 superseded ADR-013's original direct-Legacy Prototype protocol
source decision while retaining its Runtime-owned terminal-byte, REST, File
Grant and Binary WebSocket boundary. Current packaging, dependency and upstream
mapping documents therefore describe `xmodem.ts` as Axterm-authored and
`zmodem2`/`trzsz2` as separately published third-party Runtime dependencies
with pending provenance review; they no longer present a direct Legacy Prototype state
machine as product code. The documentation regression asserts this distinction
and confirms that ADR-013 is historical for source provenance. This corrects
the release-facing source claim, but does not accept IR-02/IR-03's qualified
rights review, independently operated SSH or Windows/Linux installed-app
evidence.

The P-02/P-03/P-04/P-05 replacement boundary now also has a source-snapshot
regression: `snapshot:check` rejects the former terminal-transfer directory,
local FTP Adapter, generated locale/theme snapshots and their generators if any
reappear. It deliberately leaves P-06's migration-period compatibility files to
their own transition-boundary audit, so a passing snapshot neither conceals nor
prematurely removes the old-user path. Fixture tests cover every retired input.
This strengthens clean-checkout reproducibility; it does not accept source
rights, migration, platform or release gates.

P-08 now has a later-stage final-public snapshot guard in addition to the
normal migration-period hygiene check. `bun run snapshot:final-public:check`
prepares the same source-only copy as `snapshot:check`, then scans every
retained filename and byte stream for the legacy product name, including ASCII
and UTF-16LE/BE literals at any byte offset. It rejects every
code, build, test, asset and documentation finding by default. A narrowly
recorded exception can cover only one existing `docs/` path and must name a
reason, accountable reviewer, ISO date and durable approval reference; stale
exception records fail too. The guide and blank record are
[PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_EXCEPTION_TEMPLATE](PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_EXCEPTION_TEMPLATE.md)
and [PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_EXCEPTION_TEMPLATE.json](PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_EXCEPTION_TEMPLATE.json).
On the current migration worktree, the guard correctly reports 135 unapproved
findings while the underlying hygiene audit remains clean; this expected failure
demonstrates that the guard is not being substituted for the current P-06
compatibility boundary. It is deliberately excluded from normal `bun run check`
and becomes mandatory only after IR-09 removal approval. This is an executable
final-source boundary, not rights, trademark, similarity, remote-CI,
installed-platform or IR-12/IR-14 acceptance.

A 2026-09-24 diagnostic replay of the current **902-file** prepared source
snapshot passed the ordinary hygiene audit but found **168 final-public
filename/content findings** (**2,102 occurrences**). This is expected while
the approved P-06 migration paths and historical evidence remain. The scan
now also tests UTF-16LE/BE literals, including an odd-offset embedded string;
the focused final-snapshot/release suites pass 8/8. The lower-level diagnostic
does **not** override the nine currently pending composite-release
prerequisites or authorize deleting compatibility features before the public
migration window. The full `bun run check` passes after this audit change:
1,178 unit/integration tests passed (38 skipped), 360 architecture modules /
1,310 dependencies, 267 Contract operations and all 11 visual/accessibility
journeys. IR-09 and IR-14 remain Open.

The release-facing form of that boundary is now `bun run
release:final-public:check`. Its dedicated orchestrator runs every prerequisite
and reports all blockers in one invocation instead of stopping at the first
failed command. It checks `migration:record:removal-check`, current
dependency-attribution, product-mark, four-language and public-service
reviewed/active gates; only after all prerequisites pass does it delegate to
the final-public snapshot audit. The current truthful run reports six blockers:
migration removal, dependency-attribution review, product-mark review,
navigation-catalog review, core-catalog review and active public services. It
passes the component, license-text, asset-inventory and four-language
regenerability checks, then explicitly skips the final snapshot. This prevents
a first failure from hiding the remaining owner handoffs while still refusing
to present a zero-name scan as authorized migration removal. The lower-level
snapshot command remains available only for diagnostics and focused audit
tests. This does not make the current pending record a released migration
version or accept IR-08/IR-09.

P-02 now has direct Runtime-destruction cleanup regressions across XMODEM,
ZMODEM and TRZSZ. Each selected receive first writes nonzero bytes to Axterm's
hidden staging file and then invokes the protocol session's `destroy()` without
closing its peer or terminal channel. XMODEM uses a valid CRC frame, ZMODEM uses
a real published `zmodem2` sender and TRZSZ uses a real `trzsz2` transfer peer.
All three sessions become inactive, leave neither a published destination nor a
`.part` file, and never report file/session completion. This is a narrow
source-level lifecycle-cleanup boundary for host/runtime supervision, distinct
from a malformed peer frame or channel-close case; it does not establish
independently operated external-peer interoperability, source rights,
filesystem portability, packaged-platform evidence or IR-03 acceptance. P-02
remains **In progress**.

P-02 now also resets the completed TRZSZ session's per-transfer readers,
writers and collision-name set before the terminal receives a later transfer
marker. The `TrzszManager` deliberately retains a completed session until its
terminal closes; retaining an old published writer would otherwise try to
re-publish its already removed staging path during the next download. Regressions
drive two consecutive real `trzsz2` handshakes through that same
session in both download and upload directions. They require the two distinct
binary receives to publish with two completion events and no `.part` files,
and ensure that closed first-upload readers cannot disrupt the second upload.
The shutdown path additionally leaves ordinary terminal bytes unclaimed once a
session is destroyed. This is source-level reuse and cleanup coverage—not an
external-peer, filesystem-portability, packaged-platform or source-rights
approval—so P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

P-08 now also identifies the existing `node-pty@1.1.0` Unix file-descriptor
accounting patch in the transition notice, together with the three copyright
lines from its installed MIT LICENSE. A regression ties that notice to
`patches/node-pty@1.1.0.patch`, so a future patched dependency cannot silently
be presented as unmodified Axterm-owned code. This strengthens source and
application-Legal hand-off evidence only; it does not establish the patch's
upstream lineage, actual macOS/Windows/Linux binary mapping, qualified
source-rights review or IR-02/IR-11 acceptance. P-08 remains **In progress**.
