The cross-role [release-owner handoff](RELEASE_OWNER_HANDOFF.md) now turns the
remaining legal, language, brand, migration, platform and repository actions
into explicit role-owned evidence requests. It is a coordination checklist,
not a substitute for named review or an acceptance claim: until its records
are completed under the independent matrix, all affected rows remain **In
progress** or **Open**.

The three-platform CI workflow now retains each successful unsigned candidate
installer (DMG/ZIP, NSIS EXE, AppImage/DEB and available blockmap) separately
from its platform-specific packaged SPDX sidecar. This makes the tested bytes
available to the release owner for inspection instead of retaining only an
SBOM. It also uploads a deterministic platform/product-version SHA-256
manifest for each distributable installer/archive; the SPDX sidecar covers
app resources while this manifest covers the top-level release bytes. Neither
CI artifact makes a signed/public download or IR-13 acceptance.

P-08 now also verifies that those are still the same bytes after installed-app
testing and immediately before CI upload. `release:hashes:check` regenerates
the platform/product artifact set from the release directory and compares the
stored byte counts, SHA-256 values and complete manifest policy. It rejects a
modified, added or removed distributable, duplicate path, wrong product or
platform, and extra/missing policy fields. All three workflow platforms run it
after their DMG/NSIS/DEB gates (and after Linux packaged SSH) but before the
first artifact upload. Focused tests cover the valid path plus changed bytes,
late artifacts and policy tampering. This closes a local build-to-upload
integrity gap; it is not remote-download verification, signing/notarization,
release-owner approval or IR-13 acceptance.

P-08 now closes the next workflow-defined transport gap as well. After the
installer set and matching hash manifest are uploaded, each matrix job uses the
commit-pinned `download-artifact` v4.3.0 action to retrieve those two exact
named artifacts into a clean `downloaded-release/` directory. It runs
`release:hashes:check` over the downloaded files and writes a deterministic
`AXTERM_UPLOAD_ROUNDTRIP.<platform>.json` receipt only after every selected
installer/archive byte count and SHA-256 still matches. The receipt also hashes
the downloaded manifest itself, identifies the product/platform/artifacts and
states that workflow placement—not the receipt alone—establishes the transport
origin. A separate `packaged-upload-roundtrip-<runner OS>` artifact retains it.
Focused tests execute the receipt CLI, reject overwriting a manifest/artifact,
lock all three post-download commands and their upload/download ordering, and
pin every external action by immutable commit. This is a testable remote-CI
gate definition, not evidence that a remote run has passed; it also does not
cover the separately uploaded SPDX sidecar, a public Release/CDN download,
signing/notarization, owner approval or IR-13 acceptance.

Contract operations, all source/release hygiene checks, production build/layout
and five visual plus two accessibility journeys. The candidate script removed
its temporary directories. This proves the FTP target/source identity changes
and evidence reproduce from a clean local clone; it is not the outstanding
independent security review, Windows/native-Linux installed evidence, remote
CI, signed distribution, public migration/removal or owner-created repository.
IR-04 and IR-12 remain **In progress**; IR-13 and IR-14 remain **Open**.

The newest P-08 ephemeral Git candidate supersedes the filesystem-identity
candidate after the P-03 `REST STREAM` correction. Its deliberate pre-evidence
snapshot had 751 files / 15,726,520 bytes / tree SHA-256
`75521c0b29cc3d40e90bdda600374b51d0b4289207c1934427e490ff07f3755a`;
temporary root commit `c302a43c31ac55ed2688348dc282ff34de19da22` and a
distinct `git clone --no-local` each tracked those 751 files without submodule
metadata, `vendor/legacy-prototype`, generated OpenAPI, installed dependencies or
release outputs. Frozen installation installed 609 packages and rebuilt macOS
arm64 `node-pty`; the clone repeated the full 217-file gate with five skipped
files (959 tests with 33 skips), 368 modules / 1,313 dependencies, 267 Contract
operations, all source/release hygiene checks, production build/layout and five
visual plus two accessibility journeys. The candidate script removed its
temporary directories. This proves the restart-state and atomic resumed-upload
changes reproduce from a clean local clone; it is not independent protocol/
security review, Windows/native-Linux installed evidence, remote CI, signed
distribution, public migration/removal or an owner-created repository. IR-04
and IR-12 remain **In progress**; IR-13 and IR-14 remain **Open**.

The same release workflow now pins every externally supplied GitHub Action to
the exact commit for its reviewed release (`checkout` v4.2.2, `setup-node`
v4.4.0, `setup-bun` v2.2.0, `upload-artifact` v4.6.2 and
`download-artifact` v4.3.0). A focused test
rejects mutable Action tags or a missing release annotation. This prevents a
later tag move from silently changing the clean-checkout and artifact-producing
workflow inputs. Checkout also removes its read-only token after cloning, so
dependency installation and packaging cannot reuse checkout credentials. It is
a source-supply-chain control only: no remote CI run,
Windows/Linux artifact, signing/notarization, notice/right review or IR-13
acceptance is claimed by this change.

The signed updater's unit boundary now also proves that a non-loopback HTTP
manifest is rejected before a request and that even a correctly signed artifact
is rejected when it does not share the manifest's origin. This locks down the
existing HTTPS/loopback-fixture and same-origin controls against accidental
relaxation; it is regression evidence only, not an authenticated public feed,
production signing identity, installed upgrade or IR-13 acceptance.

**IR-01 — Accepted on 2026-09-22 (governance-document review).** ADR-016 is
accepted; `MASTER_SPEC`, `AGENTS.md`, the root README, docs index, cleanup plan
and this status ledger all name independent Apache-2.0 delivery as the current
direction. The repository owner's recorded choices cover a migration-period
then final removal of compatibility paths, exactly four supported locales, a
curated independent theme set and targeted Axterm UI/UX improvements. `ADR-003`
now explicitly records its own historical/superseded state, matching the
historical status already carried by the old parity specification, roadmap,
matrix, `UPSTREAM` and harness. The focused
`independent-release-documents` regression verifies those current-document and
historical-boundary assertions. This acceptance is limited to IR-01's product
and governance evidence: the controlled archive remains required, and
contributor, source-rights, trademark and retained-asset review are still
unaccepted under IR-02.

P-01 now also has a prospective contribution boundary: `CONTRIBUTING.md` and
the PR template require a contributor to state authority to submit, Apache-2.0
submission terms, and complete disclosure of any non-original material before
maintainers merge it. The policy deliberately distinguishes future submissions
from a CLA and from retrospective clearance of historical files; it adds a
repeatable intake record but does not close the existing contributor-rights,
source-rights, trademark or human-review gates for IR-02.

P-07/IR-10 copy decoupling has started, without treating text replacement as
visual clearance. General Settings, startup/file/accessibility settings,
terminal key-sequence help, trigger empty state, theme-import confirmation,
activity-rail help and the language fallback note no longer claim Legacy Prototype
ownership or translation provenance. The fallback note now accurately says
that all four retained languages cover navigation labels while untranslated
Axterm copy uses English. `product-copy-provenance.test.ts` parses both first-party UI
catalogs and permits old-product names only in the active data-migration and
workflow-compatibility messages. A source Electron H-11 test verifies the
Chinese and English note; a copied-outside-checkout unsigned macOS arm64
`package:dir` H-11 test previously verified the English note, all 15 navigation
labels, Arabic direction and cold restart. That artifact is historical; a new
four-language package run is required after the scope reduction. This package's
`app.asar` SHA-256 is
`886e007f3d2f84dc01e6b3875c2cb609f081c802ab3f7511c53bc107ed2c9ff5`.

The new [Axterm visual baseline](AXTERM_VISUAL_BASELINE.md) now captures source
Electron Host Manager and Settings at 1280×800, 1440×900 and 1920×1080, and the
guarded Sync-format migration decision at
1280×800. Eight checked-in macOS PNGs are compared by
`tests/e2e/visual.spec.ts`; the same source journey and viewport-bound checks
run without pixel comparison on other platforms. The default `bun run check`
now includes this visual test and two keyboard/accessibility/reduced-motion
tests, and no longer includes the historical parity audit/test. The source
check at that stage passed 813 unit tests (one skipped), Level 1 and
256-operation Contract gates, the then-current 15-language/45-key catalog checks, production
build and all three Playwright tests. The previous 819-test/historical-parity
result was from the earlier gate and is not the current release result.
The historical submodule, parity scripts, reference screenshots and traces have
now been moved out of the worktree after a verified controlled archive; the
root package no longer exposes their commands, the literal-localization baseline
is independent under `tests/unit/`, and diagnostic package screenshots write
only to ignored test results. See
[HISTORICAL_REFERENCE_ARCHIVE](HISTORICAL_REFERENCE_ARCHIVE.md). The
post-removal worktree check passed 839 unit tests (one skipped), the Level 1
architecture gate, 262 Contract operations, production build and three Axterm
visual/accessibility journeys. Design review, contrast, dialogs, installed-app
visual evidence and Windows/Linux checks remain open. IR-10 remains **In
progress**.

On 2026-09-21, P-07 replaced the Renderer `legacy-prototype-parity.tokens.css` and
`legacy-prototype-shell.css` entry points with `axterm.tokens.css` and
`axterm-shell.css`. The current shell owns its palette, 48px activity rail,
38px tab strip, 32px footer, tab target range, radii, motion and menu shadow;
the former token values and geometry are no longer the active design source.
The focused style-provenance test rejects old entry points and historical
reference terms in those current CSS files. A deliberately regenerated macOS
baseline was inspected at all three Shell and Settings viewports plus the
then-supported Arabic RTL state: visible primary controls, activity rail,
selected Settings category, scrollable content and physical window controls
remain in bounds. That Arabic evidence is historical after the 2026-09-21
four-language scope revision; the current retained-language visual checks are
all LTR. This is an engineering visual review only—not a product/design,
contrast or trademark sign-off—and IR-10 remains **In progress**.

The P-07 shell now also has a deterministic semantic-contrast guard. The
Axterm-owned canvas, raised-surface, ordinary/muted text, primary-action and
danger-action tokens are checked at WCAG AA's 4.5:1 normal-text threshold.
Selected states, menus, badges and close/error controls use explicit dark
on-primary/on-danger roles rather than low-contrast white text on the bright
semantic backgrounds. This narrows a real source-color defect while leaving
rendered transparency/images, disabled state, every feature override and
cross-platform visual review as open IR-10 evidence.

On 2026-09-21, the product owner directed removal of the old irregular blue
`Axterm` empty-pane mark because it was visually too close to the historical
reference. Both empty-state render paths now omit that visual entirely; the
stylesheet no longer contains the shape or its blue gradient. The shared Shell
accent now resolves to the mint `--shell-primary`, including tab-number badges,
and current Host/profile/theme/history/import/bookmark defaults no longer use
the former blue primary literal. The AX application mark now uses a
mint-green-only gradient; `bun run icons:generate` refreshes the PNG, ICO and
ICNS variants from the editable SVG on macOS, and the asset inventory/review
handoff was regenerated for those new bytes. The owner-driven macOS
Shell/Settings baselines were regenerated and inspected through the three
source visual journeys. This is a scoped engineering UI revision, not a
trademark, cross-platform or formal design acceptance; P-07/IR-10 and P-05/
IR-07 remain **In progress**.

On 2026-09-22, the remaining bright-blue Settings category hover/selected
state (`#0099d4`) was replaced with the same mint primary and dark
on-primary roles already used by tab-number badges. The focused product-shell
provenance regression now requires both surfaces to use those semantic roles
and rejects the retired blue literals and empty-pane mark names. The focused
style/contrast tests passed four checks, and all three existing source-Electron
P-07 visual journeys passed without an intentional snapshot refresh. This is
limited source/macOS regression evidence; it neither completes brand/design or
accessibility review nor changes P-07/IR-10's **In progress** state.

The same product-color audit found an ordinary connection-profile input still
using a bright-blue border and focus halo (`#266aa2` / `#122a39`). Its default
border now blends `--shell-primary` with the shell divider, and its focus state
uses the mint primary plus a mint focus halo. The shell-style provenance test
locks that semantic rule and rejects both blue literals. This treats terminal
ANSI blue as user content rather than an Axterm chrome color; it is a narrow
P-07 source-level correction, not a visual-design, trademark or cross-platform
acceptance. P-07/IR-10 remain **In progress**.

The subsequent complete source gate passes 857 unit tests (one skipped), the
Level 1 architecture gate (365 modules / 1,301 dependencies), 267 Contract
operations, source component/license/SBOM/localization checks, production build
and the three default visual/accessibility Electron journeys.

The same P-07 pass renamed the ordinary Renderer behavior settings component,
its i18n namespace and the data-migration component, stylesheet and test
selectors to Axterm-neutral names. User-visible `legacy-prototypeData` copy, the legacy
fixture and the Runtime/API names intentionally remain during P-06's public
migration period; this is not a semantic removal of the migration path. Lint,
type checking, focused localization/provenance tests, the rebuilt source
Electron migration journey and the visual baseline pass. The subsequent complete
source gate passed 840 unit tests (one skipped), the Level 1 architecture gate,
262 Contract operations, production build and three visual/accessibility
journeys. This validates the rename but does not change the work-package states
above.

P-07 also removed historical-product wording from the active Renderer shell's
ordinary timestamp, terminal-key, tab-close, bookmark-form, connection-profile,
SSH-config and file-selection sources. The provenance test now checks these
non-migration files in addition to visible copy and the retired theme URL; the
remaining Renderer references are deliberately confined to the data/sync and
workflow compatibility flows. This remains engineering hygiene, not an
independent-design or trademark sign-off.

P-07 now has a focused 200% interface-zoom Host Manager regression. At a
1440×900 source Electron window, the actual narrow-viewport rules had left the
context sidebar overlaying the main page and intercepting Add SSH host; the
SSH editor then gave its first input only 102px of CSS width. Selecting
Bookmarks → Manage now closes that overlay at the narrow breakpoint. The
editor hides only its duplicate directory, preserves its form and protocol
choices in horizontally scrollable rows, and allocates the recovered width to
the connection fields. The visual journey checks bounded dialog/close/name
controls, Tab-driven Host address and Username visibility, and End-key access
to the final form tab; its focused source run passes. This is one real
text-scaling/dialog reflow improvement, not a complete dialog inventory,
cross-platform/packaged visual proof or reviewed IR-10 design acceptance.

The same P-07 zoom suite now covers the actual migration workspace rather than
only a compact dialog. At a 1440×900 source Electron window with 200%
BrowserWindow interface zoom, it scrolls the legacy export/import and Axterm
configuration action groups into view separately, verifies that each group
fits within the narrow viewport, and follows their real Tab order. This is
deliberately a reachability check for a long Settings page: normal scrolling
between sections remains allowed, while a scaled control may not overflow once
its section is reached. The focused source run passes. It adds a reproducible
P-07 text-scaling guard, not packaged/cross-platform visual proof, historical
data conversion proof, or reviewed IR-10 design acceptance.

P-06/P-07 now also closes a keyboard-model gap on the active sync-migration
surface. The provider and format selectors previously declared ARIA tab roles
but behaved only as ordinary click targets. They now keep only the active tab
in the sequential focus order, wrap enabled tabs with Left/Right/Up/Down,
support Home/End and select a provider's configured format when the provider
changes. The source accessibility journey uses that keyboard path across all
four providers, saves a real legacy WebDAV profile, switches into and back out
of the Axterm migration decision, and reruns the interactive-name/focus-order
audit. The 200% zoom journey reaches the same backup/create action and requires
zero horizontal panel overflow. All seven visual/accessibility Electron tests
pass. This advances keyboard and text-scaling coverage only; public migration,
live-provider recovery, installed cross-platform evidence and human IR-10
review remain open.

The normal 1280×800 macOS Axterm visual baseline now also captures Common
Settings' real data-migration panel after its Axterm configuration-export action
is brought into view. It records the transition notice, legacy import/export
controls and the independent configuration snapshot controls in one product
surface, while the visible copy continues to distinguish that bounded snapshot
from a Vault backup. This is a source-app visual regression baseline for the
migration workspace, not proof that the public migration window has begun, a
historic-data conversion test, cross-platform package evidence or IR-10 design
acceptance.

The complete default gate after the P-06 packaged prior-schema regression and
the P-07 migration-zoom addition passed on 2026-09-21: 201 test files passed
with one skipped (880 tests passed with one skipped), followed by the
367-module/1,308-dependency Level 1 architecture gate, 267 Contract operations,
component/license/attribution/SBOM/asset/localization and migration-boundary
checks, production build, and five Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. The
independent source-snapshot check also passed. These are current engineering
evidence, not acceptance of the outstanding public migration, human review,
three-platform or new-repository gates.

P-06's next Runtime/Contract boundary is now specified by
[ADR-017](../adr/ADR-017-axterm-sync-format-migration.md): a typed
`axterm-sync-v1` source must coexist with explicitly marked legacy profiles and
remote objects rather than relabeling or overwriting them. The ADR is an
implementation decision and verification plan, not evidence that IR-08's
public migration release or IR-09's later removal is complete.

The first ADR-017 implementation unit now derives `axterm-sync` v1 category
documents from the typed Axterm configuration snapshot, rather than from the
legacy portable importer. Its unit test verifies the distinct header, category
counts/hashes, omitted-secret boundary and absence of `_axterm`/credential-ref
fields.

The second ADR-017 unit makes the current sync source explicit without
mislabeling it: SQLite migration 36 adds immutable profile `format`; existing
rows and every profile created by the still-legacy Runtime are
`legacy-legacy-prototype-v1`. The output-only REST/OpenAPI field is generated into the
client, while strict create input rejects caller-supplied `axterm-sync-v1`.
Repository, HTTP-provider and real loopback WebDAV tests cover the old profile
shape; the migration test simulates a pre-36 row and proves that it receives
the legacy marker. The legacy `DataSyncService` rejects a manually marked
`axterm-sync-v1` profile before reaching a Provider/data source, and its
automatic scheduler skips that reserved format; this prevents accidental old
envelope or remote-path use while the new path is absent. This marker rollout
deliberately retains one profile per provider and does not expose `/axterm/`,
an Axterm-format provider, remote conversion or a UI selector. Those
capabilities, the `(provider, format)` constraint rebuild, preview/result
variants and cross-platform evidence remain pending. The complete source gate
now passes 845 unit tests (one skipped), the Level 1 architecture gate, 262
Contract operations, production build and three visual/accessibility journeys.

The third ADR-017 unit defines the independently named `axterm-sync` v1
document schema in Contracts and makes the Runtime verify its strict header,
nonempty allowed-category set, bounded count, SHA-256-shaped category hash and
omission metadata. It rejects non-null `*CredentialRef` values, and validates
each category's count and stable content hash before accepting it. The document
creator parses its own output through that schema; focused tests cover forged
credential references, malformed category sets and stale hashes. This makes
the future remote format identifiable and fail-closed, but it does **not** yet
upload, download, preview or import an Axterm sync document. The complete
source gate now passes 846 unit tests (one skipped), the Level 1 architecture
gate (364 modules / 1293 dependencies), 262 Contract operations, production
build and three visual/accessibility journeys.

The fourth ADR-017 unit adds `AxtermConfigurationSyncDataSource`. It derives
the independent document directly from `AxtermConfigurationService`, and, on
download, can apply only categories selected by the Profile to a fresh local
configuration snapshot before using that service's existing bounded
preview/explicit-commit transaction. It has no `Legacy PrototypeDataService` or File
Grant dependency. The test changes a remote settings category through that
path, commits it atomically, and proves a missing selected category is
rejected.

That source is now supplied by the production Runtime to `DataSyncService`.
The immutable profile format selects the legacy or independent source before a
provider is called: a marked `axterm-sync-v1` profile can compare and upload
the strict Axterm document, while a profile with no independent source remains
fail-closed. The source-selection test proves the uploaded envelope has the
`axterm-sync` header, excludes `_axterm` and credential references, and then
compares equal after re-reading it. Legacy WebDAV remains below `/legacy-prototype/`;
the Axterm profile uses `/axterm/`, and Gist/Custom use
`axterm-sync-v1.json` and the independent v1 description. The old
download-preview/commit/pending/cancel REST operations explicitly reject an
Axterm format because their response Contract is still legacy. Separate

The controlled HTTP provider suite now also exercises the Axterm-format Gist
request separately for GitHub and Gitee, including the provider-specific
authentication, `axterm-sync-v1.json` filename and independent description.
When a Gist already contains only legacy `axterm-sync.json`, the missing Axterm
file is now treated as an uncreated target and PATCH adds only
`axterm-sync-v1.json`, retaining the old sibling. Named-file Custom containers
merge the exact old and new files under an ETag-guarded PUT. A raw single-object
Custom endpoint is rejected before any Axterm PUT because it cannot safely host
both formats at one URL. Together with the existing WebDAV cases, this verifies
the non-overwrite semantics for all four built-in targets. This is controlled
source-level evidence, not a live-provider or public-migration acceptance.

The same boundary now has copied-outside-checkout macOS packaged evidence for
all four controlled providers. The production Settings UI creates legacy
GitHub Gist, Gitee Gist and Custom profiles, uses the actual Bearer/token/HS256
JWT authentication forms, saves each old payload byte-for-byte during guarded
conversion, and performs the first Axterm upload while only the legacy named
file exists. Both Gist variants retain exact `axterm-sync.json` while PATCH adds
`axterm-sync-v1.json`; the Custom PUT sends the existing ETag and contains both
exact files. Combined with the packaged WebDAV `/legacy-prototype/` versus `/axterm/`
journey, this closes the controlled macOS directory-app provider non-overwrite
gap. It is not a live account, native dialog, signed/installed artifact,
Windows/Linux package or public migration release. P-06/IR-08 remains **In
progress**.

`axterm-download-previews` and `axterm-download-commits` REST operations now
carry `AxtermConfigurationPreview` and import-result Contracts for marked
profiles; they preserve bounded preview then explicit commit rather than
coercing the independent document into legacy response fields. Raw remote
backup remains available and does not parse, mutate or overwrite the object.
Automatic Axterm downloads remain unscheduled because remote application
requires a user review and explicit commit; they cannot repeatedly create a
failed state. Risky SQLite migration 37 replaces the one-profile-per-provider
constraint with `(provider, format)`. The guarded Axterm conversion endpoint
requires a legacy ETag and writable save grant, first saves the exact old
remote object, then creates a separate auto-disabled Axterm profile; it neither
renames, overwrites nor deletes the legacy remote/profile. Shared Vault
references are retained until no sync profile references them. Repository and
service tests cover the table migration, concurrent pair, backup-before-create
ordering and credential lifetime. The complete source gate now passes 855 unit
tests (one skipped), the Level 1 architecture gate (365 modules / 1301
dependencies), 267 Contract
operations, component/license/SBOM checks, production build and three
visual/accessibility journeys.
Settings now presents isolated legacy and Axterm profile tabs per provider. A
legacy-only provider's Axterm tab offers the guarded backup-and-create action;
it does not expose a raw new-format create form. A source Electron E2E uses a
real loopback WebDAV server to upload a legacy object, save it byte-for-byte
through the native File Grant, create the separate auto-disabled Axterm
profile, assert that the legacy object is unchanged, upload only an
independent `axterm-sync` document beneath `/axterm/`, and use the Axterm
preview/explicit-commit route. The same fixture uploads a real SSH host/bookmark,
a linked Quick Command folder/two-step command, a custom terminal theme
referenced by portable settings, connection/terminal/tunnel Profiles, a linked
remote address bookmark, a named/current workspace with a referenced terminal
tab and a global cooldown trigger. Its bookmark references both reusable
Profiles, its default-terminal setting references the terminal Profile and its
tunnel references the Host. It changes the remote Axterm appearance setting,
clears the address-bookmark and workspace copies only from the remote `settings`
category and recomputes that category hash while preserving their dedicated
categories. It then deletes the local records in dependency-safe order and
resets the local selections. The reviewed commit restores all eight Axterm sync
categories without changing the legacy object or its write count. It verifies
the command's newly mapped folder reference plus both steps and metadata; a new
custom-theme ID, preserved palette and remapped selected-theme setting; new IDs
for all three Profile types with remapped bookmark, default-terminal and
tunnel/Host references; address-bookmark and two workspace-layout references
remapped to the recovered Host/bookmark/Profile/theme; the recovered startup
bookmark; and a new trigger ID with preserved match/action/cooldown fields.
Credential references remain null. This completes local recovery coverage for
the current eight-category Axterm document, not a public migration
notice/duration, historical-format recovery, live-provider proof or a migration
release. P-06 therefore stays **In progress**.

The same guarded remote-backup boundary now has a complete packaged
current-format proof: the fresh unsigned macOS arm64 directory app is copied
outside the checkout, its Desktop Host save-dialog response is deterministically
stubbed, and a dual-path loopback WebDAV server serves the old
`/legacy-prototype/desktop.json` object while reserving `/axterm/desktop.json` for the
new format. The packaged Axterm tab writes an exact local migration backup,
inherits the legacy profile's local encryption credential and uploads an
AES-256-GCM+scrypt envelope only to `/axterm/`. The remote bytes expose neither
the Host name/address, Quick Command, custom theme, workspace, trigger or the
encryption password. The fixture uploads all eight categories with linked
Host/bookmark, Quick Command tree, custom theme, connection/terminal/tunnel
Profiles, address bookmark, named/current workspace, trigger and portable
settings, then deletes every local record in dependency-safe order and clears
the settings copies. Packaged preview and explicit commit decrypt the same
remote object and restore all eight independent categories under new record IDs.
The bookmark, tunnel, terminal setting, address bookmark, current/named
workspace layouts and startup selection point at the matching recovered
Host/bookmark/Profile/theme IDs, and credential references remain null. The old
object remains byte-exact with two reads and zero writes; the Axterm object
receives one encrypted upload and no download-side write. This proves
checkout-independent macOS directory-app backup, encrypted isolated upload and
reviewed eight-category decryption/recovery, not a real native-dialog
interaction, signing or installation evidence, Windows/Linux coverage,
live-provider compatibility, old-release recovery, a public notice or the
duration decision. P-06/IR-08 remains **In progress**.

The migration-period `legacy-prototype://` path now also carries an in-memory-only
legacy-scheme marker from the shared parser to Renderer. It displays a
dismissible notice that the link must be recreated as `axterm://` before the
public migration window ends. The marker is deliberately omitted from REST,
SQLite, logs and browser storage. The source Electron F-08 journey and a fresh
unsigned macOS arm64 `package:dir` journey both deliver the same FTP link under
the legacy and native schemes, verify equivalent prefilled form values, verify
that only the legacy scheme shows the notice, and confirm it can be dismissed
above the open form. This provides a truthful UI warning, not a public stable
release, migration-duration decision, signed/installed artifact, old-release
recovery or Windows/Linux evidence; P-06/IR-08 stays **In progress**.

P-06 then had an executable transition-boundary ledger, now retained in the
controlled historical evidence archive.
Its 30-file production-source allowlist contains only legacy data/link/sync and
command-line-workflow compatibility plus negative build/snapshot guards; `bun run
transition:boundaries:check` is part of the normal check and rejects a new
unreviewed production reference or a boundary change without a corresponding
ledger/test update. Four historical behavior comments outside this temporary
surface were rewritten as Axterm-neutral technical descriptions. This prevents
scope expansion during migration; it does not begin the public migration
window, approve source rights, or change P-06/IR-08 **In progress** and
IR-09 **Open** states.

The new P-08 `candidate:check` automation is one of those explicit negative
guards: it only asserts `vendor/legacy-prototype` is absent from its disposable
independent snapshot and `git clone --no-local` candidate. Its first complete
run exposed the missing allowlist entry, so the boundary audit correctly failed
closed until this 30-file ledger/document update was made. That containment
update does not enlarge the product compatibility surface or begin a migration
window.

The boundary review also found the bounded legacy command-line workflow array
behind `--batch-op`. It already discarded inline credentials, required an
existing saved SSH bookmark and rejected file-transfer actions without a File
Grant, but it had not been recorded as a P-06 migration surface or warned the
user after a successful conversion. It is now a 29th explicitly guarded source
file: the parser returns only an in-memory `legacy-legacy-prototype-workflow` marker,
and Renderer displays a dismissible migration-period notice after creating the
native Axterm batch operation. Unit and source Electron coverage verify native
and legacy paths, secret exclusion and dismissal. This expands migration
coverage without treating an old workflow format as a permanent Axterm API.
The boundary audit's three focused tests and the complete source gate passed on
2026-09-21: 197 test files passed with one skipped (869 tests passed with one
skipped), followed by the 365-module/1,301-dependency architecture gate, 267
Contract operations, component/license/SBOM/asset/localization gates, the
production build and three Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. This is a
current source result, not a public migration release or platform acceptance.

P-08/IR-12 has a partial independence result, not a clean-checkout sign-off.
On 2026-09-20 a new source copy at `/tmp/axterm-independent-check.GmD68b`
excluded `.git`, `vendor`, installed modules, build outputs and test results;
both `.git` and `vendor` were verified absent. In that copy,
`bun install --frozen-lockfile` and the then-current `bun run check` both exited
successfully with the same 813-unit/three-Playwright gate. Standard CI now
checks out without recursive submodules and prepares Linux Xvfb before running
`check`. The copy is not an actual new Git checkout or CI run; real protocol
fixtures, full desktop E2E, installed package/upgrade and Windows/Linux
evidence remain separate. IR-12 is **In progress**, not Accepted.
After the historical reference material was physically moved out of the
worktree, a second temporary source copy at
`/tmp/axterm-independent-current.9uoPMz` explicitly excluded `.git`,
`.gitmodules`, `vendor/legacy-prototype`, installed modules, build/release and test
outputs, and `.env*`; every excluded path was asserted absent before the frozen
install. Its `bun install --frozen-lockfile` rebuilt native dependencies and

Level 1 architecture gate, 262 Contract operations, production build and three
Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. This is stronger current no-submodule
source evidence only: it is still an uncommitted temporary copy, not a new Git

On 2026-09-21, a fresh current source copy at
`/tmp/axterm-independent-20260921.O9YS04` excluded `.git`, `.gitmodules`,
`vendor/`, installed modules, build/release/test output and `.env*`; its
pre-install assertions verified that `.git`, `.gitmodules`,
`vendor/legacy-prototype`, `node_modules` and `out` were absent. Frozen installation
rebuilt `node-pty` on macOS arm64, and the complete source check passed: 855
unit tests (one skipped), 365 modules / 1,301 dependencies, 267 Contract
operations, source component/license/SBOM/localization gates, production build
and the three default visual/accessibility Electron journeys. The durable
[IR-12 source-copy evidence](evidence/IR12-INDEPENDENT-COPY-2026-09-21.md)
records the exact exclusion set and limits. It is stronger current
no-submodule evidence, but remains an uncommitted file copy rather than a new
Git checkout, CI result, installed platform or distribution acceptance;
IR-12 stays **In progress**.

P-08 now has a reusable independent-snapshot audit rather than relying only on
the preceding manual exclusion list. `bun run audit:independent-snapshot --
--snapshot <path>` walks a prepared source copy deterministically, hashes its
sorted source manifest and rejects Git/submodule/parity material, dependency
or build/test output, non-example `.env` files, symbolic links and the retired
direct dependency markers. Its migration-aware scope intentionally does not
claim that retained P-06 compatibility strings have already passed IR-09. A
fresh macOS arm64 source copy passed with 671 files / 22,699,607 bytes and tree
SHA-256 `a73550441d5d5903a0ae094c7e3fbbdaae1b3173479f5c2aa3a114bcffcc2463`;
frozen installation rebuilt `node-pty`, then its complete gate passed 859 unit
tests (one skipped), 365 modules / 1,301 dependencies, 267 Contract operations
and the default build/visual-accessibility checks. The [durable evidence
record](evidence/IR12-INDEPENDENT-COPY-2026-09-21.md) includes the exact limits:
this is not an owner-created Git repository, CI run, source-rights sign-off,
installed-package result or IR-12 acceptance.

P-08 now has a portable `snapshot:check` pre-install gate in GitHub Actions.
It creates a temporary source-only copy while excluding Git/submodule metadata,
the `vendor/` tree, installed dependencies, build/release/test output,
historical parity material and non-example `.env*` files, then runs the
existing deterministic independent-snapshot audit. CI runs it on macOS, Ubuntu
and Windows before frozen installation. The workflow's ordinary checkout,
frozen install and complete check still exercise the committed source; this
new gate enforces copy hygiene, but intentionally does not claim that the
temporary copy has itself completed a second install/build or that IR-12 is
accepted.

The working tree's direct `bun run snapshot:check` is a required current
hygiene assertion: it reports its source-file count, byte count and
content-dependent tree hash at execution, then rejects forbidden entries,
environment files, source files over 5 MiB, symbolic links, high-confidence
textual private-key/token/test-credential formats, Playwright-style trace/HAR/
recording artifacts, the on-demand `docs/api/openapi.json` intermediary,
common binary credential/key-store containers, SSH private-key filenames,
local SQLite/database state and sidecars, and direct/lockfile dependency
violations. It reports only a violating path and credential/artifact class,
never a matched value. The focused audit/preparation/final-public suites pass
9/9 and the current prepared source reports zero sensitive artifacts. `bun run
check` includes this assertion, so the normal local gate uses the same
pre-repository hygiene controls as CI.
Because this status file is itself part of the audited source tree, a fixed
hash copied here would become stale whenever the ledger changes; release
evidence must instead preserve the command output alongside the final frozen
tree. A passing local command is not a fresh independent checkout,
source-rights clearance, full secret-history/content review, package test or
IR-12 acceptance.

The same portable preparation path has now produced a passing macOS arm64
isolated copy that completed `bun install --frozen-lockfile` and the full gate:
865 tests passed (one skipped), with the 365-module / 1,301-dependency
architecture gate, 267 Contract operations, component/license/SBOM/product-
asset inventories, localization, production build/layout and all three
visual/accessibility journeys. This validates the new copy mechanism beyond
CI's pre-install audit, while still remaining uncommitted source-copy evidence
rather than a new repository, signed release or Windows/Linux installation.

P-08/IR-12 now also has a local ephemeral Git-candidate proof, documented in
[IR12-EPHEMERAL-GIT-CANDIDATE-2026-09-21](evidence/IR12-EPHEMERAL-GIT-CANDIDATE-2026-09-21.md).
It found that three Finder `.DS_Store` files had passed the earlier source-copy
audit while Git correctly ignored them, so audit count and initial commit count
did not agree. The preparation/audit code now excludes `.DS_Store`, `Thumbs.db`
and `desktop.ini`, and treats any such file already in a candidate as a
violation. A corrected 699-file snapshot became a synthetic local root commit,
then a separately cloned clean/no-submodule checkout; its frozen install and
complete check passed with 881 tests and one skip, the 367-module/1,308-
dependency architecture gate, 267 Contract operations and five desktop
visual/accessibility journeys. This proves a stronger local candidate-repo
reproduction path, not an owner-created remote repository, CI run, legal
clearance, signed distribution, migration release or IR-12/IR-14 acceptance.

The candidate above predates the final large-generated-artifact rule. Its
tracked tree still contained the generated 8.4 MiB `docs/api/openapi.json`, so
it is retained only as a reproducibility probe and must not be reused as the
final source candidate. That OpenAPI document is now ignored and generated on
demand by `bun run openapi:generate`; `bun run client:generate` and
`bun run contracts:check` derive the client directly from the in-memory Runtime
contract, so the generated JSON is no longer required for correctness or
client-drift verification. New snapshot audits reject every source file larger
than 5 MiB and high-confidence complete PEM private-key/common-token formats.
Focused unit coverage verifies both failure paths and a current snapshot
passes with no such findings. This narrows accidental-release exposure without
claiming a complete secret, copyright, trademark or source-rights review; a
fresh final candidate and its independent checkout remain required.

That post-control replacement candidate is now recorded in the same

Its prepared 699-file, 15,139,997-byte snapshot had no hygiene, oversized-file
or high-confidence-secret findings before installation. A synthetic local root
commit and a separate `git clone --no-local` had the same 699 tracked files,
no submodule, and no tracked generated OpenAPI document; the new ignore rule
was checked without consulting old index state. Frozen installation and the
complete 881-pass/one-skip source gate then passed in that clone, which remained
clean after the check. It is the strongest current local IR-12 evidence, but
not an owner-created remote repository, external CI, source-rights approval or
IR-12/IR-14 acceptance.

An intervening source check exposed a flaky test-only SSH host-key fixture:
`ssh2@1.17.0` generated Ed25519 private keys that its own parser rejected in
13 of 2,000 local samples. The transfer fixture now uses ECDSA P-256 for its
encrypted SSH PTY channel; it does not change product host-key behavior. Its
four focused protocol tests and the subsequent complete source and updated
no-`vendor` copy checks all pass. This removes one test flake, not the external
SSH interoperability and installed-platform evidence still required by IR-03.

The isolated copy has since passed the broader
[IR-12 macOS no-submodule evidence](evidence/IR12-MACOS-INDEPENDENT-2026-09-20.md):
73 desktop journeys with 11 platform/fixture skips; an unsigned macOS arm64
directory app and 14 packaged journeys with nine conditional skips; real
Docker OpenSSH integration plus source and packaged SSH journeys; and a valid
unsigned DMG whose app ran after mount, independent copy and detach. Two
full-suite failures exposed stale E2E expectations for Axterm's renamed theme
success message and Settings tab; both assertions were updated to the current
visible UI, with focused and complete reruns passing. Artifact hashes and
coverage limits are in the linked record. No committed clean checkout,
Windows/Linux installer, production signing or final source-rights clearance
is implied; IR-12/IR-13/IR-14 remain unaccepted.

P-08/IR-11 now has a deterministic
[production-component index](evidence/IR11-COMPONENT-INDEX-2026-09-20.md):
125 name/version/license entries from the frozen Bun production graph are
checked by the default gate, packaged outside ASAR and displayed byte-for-byte
in About. The updated macOS arm64 package has 89 ASAR manifests (three Axterm
workspace packages); all 86 third-party identities and declared licenses are
covered by the index, and the inventory hashes 12 external legal files. A
no-`vendor` unsigned DMG verifies, mounts, copies and detaches; its installed
app passes 14 packaged journeys with nine platform/fixture skips. During the
first DMG run, a ZMODEM test peer started on unrelated PTY input before the
explicit test handshake. The peer now waits for `!`; a new real-PTY regression,
five focused packaged repeats and the complete DMG rerun pass. Artifact hashes
and remaining limits are in the linked record. The latest source `bun run check`
passes 817 unit tests (one skipped) and three visual/accessibility journeys.
This closes only the component
**metadata** gap, not exact per-component notices, an artifact SBOM,
LGPL/MPL obligations, Windows/Linux or qualified rights review. IR-02 and
IR-11 remain **In progress**.

P-08/IR-11 also has a

125 installed production package entries; six packages lacking a root file
are explicit review items. The default `check` rejects archive drift. A

unsigned macOS arm64 directory packaging. Its archive matches the source
byte-for-byte; all actual ASAR package root-license hashes match the archive,
and three examples are readable in About. The complete packaged suite and a
verified/mounted/copied/detached unsigned DMG each passed 14 journeys with
nine conditional skips; the latest source check passed 820
unit tests (one skipped) and three visual/accessibility journeys. This does
not establish complete file-level notices, source-to-binary SBOM, LGPL/MPL
compliance, Windows/Linux package evidence or legal sign-off. IR-02/IR-11
remain **In progress**.

On 2026-09-21, P-08 added `AXTERM_PRODUCTION_DEPENDENCIES.spdx.json`, a
deterministic SPDX 2.3 rendering of the frozen Bun production dependency graph.
It has 125 package identities, is guarded by `bun run sbom:check`, included in
the builder's external legal files and rendered in About alongside the
component index and license texts. Its scope and `NOASSERTION` license fields
explicitly avoid treating package metadata as file-level clearance. The source
inventory is useful release evidence but is not the required macOS/Windows/Linux
artifact SBOM. The subsequent complete source gate passed 841 unit tests (one
skipped), the Level 1 architecture gate, 262 Contract operations, production
build and three visual/accessibility journeys; IR-02 and IR-11 stay **In
progress**.

The [six missing-root-license follow-up](evidence/IR11-MISSING-ROOT-LICENSES-2026-09-20.md)
preserves the exact official Drizzle ORM 0.45.2 Apache text in the source,

the official tag; this is sampled lineage, not a full source-to-binary map.
The existing xterm.js 6.0.0 MIT text is now also identified for
`@xterm/headless`, with its publish-build version discrepancy left open.
Both matching Hono middleware source tags and published packages omit root
license files, so their exact notice/rights review remains open. The
generated archive still reports all six package-internal omissions. A fresh
no-`vendor` macOS arm64 `package:dir` app includes the Drizzle text and
updated notice byte-for-byte, exposes both in About, and passes 14 packaged
journeys with nine conditional skips. Its inventory has 14 external legal
files and still reports both ASAR packages without internal root LICENSE
files. The latest source `bun run check` passes 825 unit tests (one skipped),
architecture/Contract/license/localization/build gates and three
visual/accessibility journeys. IR-02
and IR-11 stay **In progress**.

P-05/IR-07 asset inventory is now recorded in
[PRODUCT_ASSET_PROVENANCE](PRODUCT_ASSET_PROVENANCE.md). The current macOS
`package:dir` app's icon matches the source `icon.icns` byte-for-byte; bundled
Maple Mono WOFF/WOFF2 match their installed source files, and the font's OFL
text is in the transition notice. The derived icon set still needs
creator/permission attestation and independent brand/trademark review. `bun run
icons:generate` now records the macOS SVG-to-variant process,
but it does not establish a rights conclusion or Windows/Linux package proof. The
old upstream reference screenshots are historical evidence and remain in the
working tree pending Axterm's own visual gate and final snapshot cleanup.
Windows/Linux asset contents are unverified. IR-07 is **In progress**, not
Accepted.

The P-05 asset ledger is now also machine-readable: `AXTERM_PRODUCT_ASSETS.json`
records the exact byte size and SHA-256 of the editable SVG, Linux PNGs,
Windows ICO and macOS ICNS. The unused documentation-only design concept was
removed rather than retained with an unresolved public disposition.
`assets:check` regenerates that deterministic inventory in memory during `bun
run check` and fails on a missing or changed declared asset. The packaged
Legal/About journey now hashes the copied macOS application's
`Contents/Resources/icon.icns` and compares it to the ledger. This makes
source-to-mac-package drift reviewable; it does not make a content hash into a
creator attestation, trademark review, deterministic ICO/ICNS production record
or Windows/Linux package proof.

P-05 now also has `PRODUCT_ASSET_REVIEW_LEDGER.json`, a separate
machine-readable review queue bound to the exact five declared product-mark
asset hashes. `assets:marks:check` runs in the normal source gate and rejects
stale scope or malformed review claims; the release-only
`assets:marks:reviewed-check` additionally requires a rights review, brand
review, ISO dates, evidence, conclusion and final retain/exclude disposition
for each current byte. All records deliberately remain pending. The command is
limited to first-party product marks: it does not clear fonts,
Electron/Chromium, renderer bundles, native/WASM assets, platform packaging or
IR-07. This makes the human handoff mechanically enforceable without
manufacturing a rights conclusion; IR-07 remains **In progress**.

The retired documentation-only product-asset location is now an executable
source-boundary rule: `snapshot:check` rejects `docs/product/assets/` if it is
reintroduced, rather than silently excluding it from the prepared candidate.
The focused snapshot fixture verifies that behavior. This keeps an unreviewed
documentation image from evading the five-file application-mark inventory and
review queue, but does not constitute a creator, rights, trademark or
cross-platform package review; P-05/IR-07 remain **In progress**.

The published package omits a root license file but declares
`MIT OR Apache-2.0`; the transition notice identifies the selected Apache

clipboard regression and Windows/Linux package checks remain open. Module
initialization is not RDP interoperability or legal clearance; IR-02/IR-11
remain **In progress**.
The post-replacement `bun run check` passes 817 tests (one skipped), Level 1
architecture, 256-operation Contract, 15-language navigation catalog, source

P-04's [scope and provenance record](LOCALIZATION_SCOPE_AUDIT.md) found 45
distinct Renderer `t()` keys behind 50 literal and five fixed dynamic calls;
the former 15 × 410 Legacy Prototype-derived snapshot contained only 42 of those keys
and 368 keys not referenced by Renderer `t()`. A new 15-language, 45-key
Axterm navigation catalog is now generated deterministically from Axterm UI
semantics and AI-assisted translation drafts without reading
`vendor/legacy-prototype`. The old generated snapshot and upstream generator are
removed from the working tree; Runtime UI uses the new catalog. `locales:scope`
now requires 45/45 used keys, with no missing/unused key or unreviewed dynamic
call. Unit and source Electron H-11 tests pass; a copied macOS arm64
`package:dir` app passes immediate switching through **all 15 language IDs**,
English cold-restart persistence and Arabic RTL. The separate Axterm `x()`
catalog still has 2,492 English/Simplified Chinese keys with English fallback
for other selections. No human language, copyright or brand reviewer has
signed off the drafts, and Windows/Linux packaged evidence is missing.
The [localization fallback policy](LOCALIZATION_FALLBACK_POLICY.md) now fixes
the current navigation-versus-core-copy boundary, truthful release wording,
translation provenance/review workflow and per-scope promotion rules. It makes
the English fallback observable and supportable without describing it as
complete 15-language coverage; it does not replace the independent human or
platform reviews required for IR-05.

`01e74cb4dea63aa657e459b76cf746256946fd47b02c543bb72b66049dd76cf2`.
The final post-change `bun run check` passes 816 tests (one skipped), Level 1
architecture, 256-operation Contract, 15 × 45 catalog/drift/scope, Renderer
localization, historical parity and production-build gates; that parity pass
does not replace independent-release acceptance.
P-04/IR-05 are **In progress**, not Accepted; the historical parity pass does
not certify independent source or complete localization.

P-04 now also records its 15 navigation drafts in the machine-readable
`scripts/localization/axterm-navigation-review-ledger.json`. The generator
checks its exact 44-key Renderer-scope SHA-256, one record per supported
locale, transparent AI-assisted/source-language draft metadata, author-record
status and separate language/rights review states. A locale cannot claim
`reviewed` without recorded authorship plus named reviewers and ISO dates for
both reviews; a changed navigation scope makes the review ledger stale. All
15 records deliberately remain pending with attribution unrecorded, so this is
an enforceable hand-off to human reviewers—not a manufactured P-04 approval.
Focused ledger tests plus `locales:check`/`locales:draft` pass, while IR-05
remains **In progress** pending the human, visible-copy and packaged-platform
evidence listed above. The complete post-ledger source gate passes 197 test
files with one skipped (869 tests with one skipped), the 365-module/1,301-
dependency architecture gate, 267 Contract operations, source supply-chain
checks, production build and three Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. This
does not replace human or supported-platform evidence.

P-04 now also has the explicit, release-facing
`bun run locales:navigation:reviewed-check` gate. It rejects a stale generated
navigation catalog or any locale without recorded draft attribution and both
qualified language/rights sign-offs, rather than allowing a release process to
mistake `locales:draft`'s informational pending list for approval. The command
is deliberately limited to the 44-key navigation scope: it cannot certify the
broader `x()` copy, product wording or supported packaged platforms, so it does
not alter IR-05's **In progress** status.

On 2026-09-21, the owner changed the retained-language product scope from the
earlier 15-language draft to English, Japanese, Simplified Chinese and
Traditional Chinese. `APP_LANGUAGES`, the selector, the editable and generated
navigation catalogs, navigation-review ledger and core-copy review ledger now
contain exactly those four IDs; the other 11 locale entries have been removed.
The current-delivery section of `MASTER_SPEC` now records the same four-language
scope, preventing a superseded 15-language statement from being read as an
active requirement.
Migration 38 safely resets an already persisted removed locale to English and
creates the standard recoverable pre-migration database backup. The Contract
rejects removed language IDs, while browser locale resolution falls back to
English. Arabic is no longer a product locale and no retained locale is RTL.
The execution plan and localization audit now make the release boundary
explicit: only `en`, `ja`, `zh-CN` and `zh-TW` are selectable product/Contract
IDs. Chinese regional-preference normalization is usability mapping only, not
an additional locale or catalog; reintroducing any language requires an ADR
and fresh provenance, review, accessibility and packaged-platform evidence.

P-03's independently owned FTP server now also has a bounded system-`curl`
active-data regression: after the existing passive list/upload/download path,
`curl --ftp-port -` uploads and downloads a separate byte-exact binary through
the server's same-control-peer `PORT`/`EPRT` route. A fresh unsigned macOS
arm64 `package:dir` app copied outside the checkout passes that same journey;
its ASAR SHA-256 is
`37e4dceaf488390783ef10053982d2b9366462b319ac2fa45511267e541b9b0c`.
This is source and macOS directory-package external-client interoperability
evidence, not a signed/installed Windows/Linux, DMG active-mode, security or
source-rights conclusion; IR-04 remains **In progress**.

The broader literal `x()` catalog has 2,543 exact matching English and
Simplified-Chinese keys. It now supports per-key fallback rather than treating a
locale as all-or-nothing: Japanese and Traditional Chinese each have 2,168
AI-assisted Axterm drafts for the language Settings surface, common actions,
high-frequency Shell controls, the Settings scaffold, migration-period
data-import and legacy-sync surfaces, and terminal-workspace search, paste
review, reconnect, shortcut-bar, dropped-file and transfer-status controls,
plus startup, file-management, external-editor and accessibility settings, the
connection-profile workspace, the terminal-profile editor, and the complete
hosts/SSH-bookmark workflow, including connection stability, connection security,
connection dialogs and status/error reporting; and the FTP, Telnet, serial, RDP,
VNC, SPICE and Web bookmark editor workflow; and the complete terminal
workspace/context-menu workflow, including transfer, logging, clipboard,
selection, input-limit, rendering-fallback and OSC 52 safety feedback; and the
sync-service, credential, category, comparison and migration-period workflow;
and the Axterm-owned configuration snapshot export, inspection, preview and

safe-field editing, atomic import and result-report workflow; and the batch SSH
operation editor, concurrency, execution state and per-host result workflow;

activity-rail ordering and fixed-tool availability; static-file, local FTP and

remote-desktop connection, display and security feedback; and connection-history

complete 174-key file-manager surface: transfer, FTP capability, permission,
drag-and-drop, remote copy, primary actions, local and remote browsing,
queue/conflict handling and path validation; and the complete 163-key global
Shell surface: status, recovery, deep links, connection lifecycle, workspaces,
tab actions, Runtime state, AI context and transfer center; and the complete
91-key AI workspace surface for provider configuration, conversations,
attachments, command review, bounded/redacted context, tool activity, risk and

surfaces, including secret exclusion, explicit review-before-save, host-key

quick-command library and multi-step command editor surface, preserving
clipboard placeholders, explicit send-versus-insert behavior and the rule that

AI-Inspector, approval-card and changed-host-key confirmation surface; and the

The machine-checked
`scripts/localization/axterm-core-review-ledger.json` binds every retained
locale's exact message hash and records those two locales as **partial** (2,168
of 2,543), not complete or English-only fallback. All source-attribution,
language and rights-review records remain pending. `bun run locales:core:check`
is part of `bun run check` and rejects catalog drift or a false coverage claim;
`bun run locales:core:reviewed-check` deliberately remains red until each
retained language has complete coverage, recorded attribution and independent
language/rights reviewers. This narrow, unreviewed draft improves actual
Settings and Shell usability; it does not promote IR-05 or claim
native-language coverage of the wider application copy. Historical 15-language
and Arabic evidence above remains retained only as chronological evidence.

The source H-11 Electron journey and a copied-outside-checkout macOS arm64

and assert their respective partial-coverage Settings text. This confirms the

package evidence needed for IR-05.

P-04 then added the complete 91-key AI workspace surface to both partial

acceptance.

P-04 then added the remaining 74 AI SSH-bookmark, known-host-key and SSH-tunnel
entries to both partial catalogs, raising coverage to 1,817 of 2,543 keys each.
The localizations keep the security boundaries visible: bookmark drafts exclude
passwords, private keys, passphrases, API keys and tokens; every generated field
must be reviewed before saving; trusted host keys can be revoked only with a
next-connection fingerprint recheck; and non-loopback tunnel binding remains an
explicitly labeled choice. All placeholders, host/port pairs and state labels are
preserved. The entries remain AI-assisted drafts with attribution, language and
rights review pending; they do not change the SSH host-key, Vault, approval or
IR-05 acceptance policy.

P-04 then added all 63 quick-command library and multi-step command-editor
entries to both partial catalogs, raising coverage to 1,880 of 2,543 keys each.
The translations preserve `{{clipboard}}` literally; distinguish insertion into
the current input line from explicit Send-and-run; say that Enter is required to
run an inserted command; and state that a multi-step insertion creates text but
never silently executes it. This remains AI-assisted draft copy with
attribution, language and rights review pending, not a change to command
approval/execution policy or an IR-05 acceptance.

P-04 then added all 45 productivity-panel, AI-Inspector, approval-card and

retain approval and argument-hash labels, and require an explicit confirmation
when replacing a changed SSH host key. Attribution, language and rights review
remain pending; this is no approval-policy, host-trust-policy or IR-05 status
change.

P-04 then added all 50 saved-credential and SSH-proxy configuration entries to

proxy execution policy or accept IR-05.

P-04 then added all 69 keyboard-shortcut configuration and global

P-04 then added all 41 terminal-information panel entries to both partial

P-04 then added all 40 terminal-interaction and recovery-settings entries to

reproducibility evidence for unreviewed draft content only, not language/rights
review, public migration, remote CI, signed or installed cross-platform release
evidence, or IR-05/IR-12/IR-14 acceptance.

The P-04 command-history and bookmark-trigger candidate supersedes the
bookmark-tree candidate. Its deliberate pre-evidence snapshot had 729 files /
15,774,854 bytes / source-tree SHA-256
`36f8b910497067c9f4737d5d70437e32a806081f779a69e6c112f7d266eded39`; its
disposable root commit
`c08a93e73e1709e1919b0bd73fffb671ea04b488` and distinct `git clone --no-local`
each tracked 729 clean, no-submodule files with no `vendor/legacy-prototype` or generated
OpenAPI. Frozen installation rebuilt macOS arm64 `node-pty`; the clone's complete
`bun run check` passed 211 test files (902 tests) with one skip, the 367-module /
1,308-dependency Level 1 gate, 267 Contract operations, four-catalog /
2,543-key localization checks, commercialization checks, production build/layout
and five Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64
`package:dir` H-11 journey also passed immediate retained-language switching and
cold-restart persistence. The 2,386-key drafts retain the command-history
default-off, explicitly submitted safe single-line-command and insert-then-Enter
boundaries, and bookmark triggers' per-bookmark terminal scope, coexistence with
global rules and Runtime regular-expression safety revalidation. This is local
reproducibility evidence for unreviewed draft content only, not language/rights
review, public migration, remote CI, signed or installed cross-platform release
evidence, or IR-05/IR-12/IR-14 acceptance.

The P-04 complete-core-draft candidate supersedes the command-history and
bookmark-trigger candidate. Its deliberate pre-evidence snapshot had 729 files /
15,807,663 bytes / source-tree SHA-256
`32534ce012e0146d55a3a62e03b40c973069c9cb56271be364d82f215e44f4d0`; its
disposable root commit
`e36bc833d8bd74bca566fb2f4949439cc4c8cc50` and distinct `git clone --no-local`
each tracked 729 clean, no-submodule files with no `vendor/legacy-prototype` or generated
OpenAPI. Frozen installation rebuilt macOS arm64 `node-pty`; the clone's complete
`bun run check` passed 211 test files (902 tests) with one skip, the 367-module /
1,308-dependency Level 1 gate, 267 Contract operations, four-catalog /
2,543-key localization checks with no partial core locales or English fallback,
commercialization checks, production build/layout and five Axterm
visual/accessibility journeys. A fresh unsigned macOS arm64 `package:dir` H-11
