The source H-11 Electron journey and the copied macOS arm64 `package:dir`
H-11 journey have both been rerun against the four-language scope. They cycle
the retained selector IDs, assert exact navigation labels and `html.lang`, keep
LTR direction, confirm English cold-restart persistence, and now open the
Japanese and Traditional-Chinese shortcut panel to assert its title,
physical-key hint and action-registry table. This gives current source and
unsigned macOS directory-package regression evidence only;
Windows/Linux packages, installed release evidence, language/rights review and
full core-copy coverage remain required for IR-05.

The transition build now has a source `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` naming the known
Legacy Prototype-derived material, `zmodem2`, `trzsz2`, `spice-client`, noVNC and Maple
Mono, with applicable MIT and OFL text. A production-license inventory reports
119 package records and identifies `spice-client@1.2.0` as
LGPL-3.0-or-later and `@novnc/novnc@1.7.0` as MPL-2.0; their packages contain
additional provenance and file-level notices. Exact GNU GPL/LGPL and noVNC
MPL/BSD/DES texts are now in `licenses/`, with provenance and outstanding
obligations in [THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT](THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT.md).
`electron-builder` places those documents, the notice and Axterm's Apache-2.0
`LICENSE` outside `app.asar`. Settings → About and licenses displays the same
source text. A rebuilt macOS arm64 `package:dir` build copied each file
byte-for-byte, and packaged Playwright tests compared every displayed document
with its source. This is **not** a complete transitive dependency notice/SBOM
or a decision that LGPL/MPL distribution obligations are met; Windows/Linux
packaged verification and qualified rights review remain open. P-08, IR-02 and
IR-11 remain in progress.

The separately published `zmodem2` README identifies it as a port of the
Rust `zmodem2` crate. That crate's MIT notice names Alexey Arbuzov and Jarkko
Sakkinen in addition to the JS package's own ZHAO Xudong notice. The source
`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt` now preserves all three attribution lines, with a
test guarding them. A rebuilt macOS arm64 `package:dir` app contains the
notice byte-for-byte (SHA-256
`06ee526731440eba42b356b409b5329ca9f7a7b170693353026d0b2071f32393`),
and packaged About/notice tests pass; its `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`8c94a4a71b28be8c844e5a95b451bfa196bd71a8f9d92401d534cdd44e2a262b`.
The exact translated-code lineage and third-party obligations still require
qualified review, so this does not close P-08/IR-02/IR-11.

A new `licenses:packaged:inventory` command inspects the actual ASAR plus
unpacked files and external legal documents without calling that inventory a
complete SBOM. The initial macOS arm64 artifact exposed 25 native `.node`
binaries, including foreign-platform prebuilds. Both native modules now omit
published `prebuilds` directories from the package and use their rebuilt
Electron-ABI binaries. The rebuilt macOS artifact has 89 package manifests,
12 renderer assets and 204 unpacked files, including four `.node` binaries;
neither ASAR nor the unpacked tree contains prebuild paths. A copied-outside-
checkout packaged journey passed local PTY, search, serial enumeration,
SQLite and restart; a packaged inventory test guards the file exclusion.
Windows/Linux artifacts remain unverified. The first inventory version flagged
absent singular `license` fields for `buildcheck` and `ssh2` (both ship a root
LICENSE and declare MIT through the older `licenses` field) and a
missing published root license file for `@xterm/addon-serialize`. The command's
nested-package/notice-hash fixture test passes. The exact artifact hash,
reproduction command and follow-ups are recorded in
[THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT](THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT.md). This narrows the
review queue but does not close IR-02/IR-11.

The packaged inventory now distinguishes the Electron runtime's own legal
materials from Axterm's copied `extraResources`: it locates
`LICENSE.electron.txt` and `LICENSES.chromium.html` beneath the artifact root,
records artifact-relative paths and hashes, and the macOS directory-package test
compares both to Electron 44.3.0's installed distribution bytes. The current
macOS hashes are `5154e165bd6c2cc0cfbcd8916498c7abab0497923bafcd5cb07673fe8480087d`
for Electron's MIT text and
`a62dabd1c6ef1327365b2a3fdffb806222684a746dcb8f4afd1c1f690eba5535` for
Chromium's notice HTML; see
[IR-11 Electron runtime license materials](evidence/IR11-ELECTRON-RUNTIME-LICENSES-2026-09-21.md).
Settings → About now exposes these exact two documents only, lazily through a
fixed `axterm-license:` Electron Main protocol and an empty-sandbox iframe.
The resolver accepts no arbitrary path or network URL; the focused macOS
packaged test reads Electron's text and verifies Chromium's streamed `200`
response, while the source Desktop security journey retains a narrow
`frame-src axterm-license:` CSP rule. This makes a previously untracked
packaged legal boundary auditable; it does not make the inventory a complete
Electron/Chromium SBOM, expose every runtime notice, or close qualified rights
and Windows/Linux review.
The complete source gate after this inventory change passes 197 test files with
one skipped (870 tests with one skipped), the 365-module/1,301-dependency
architecture gate, 267 Contract operations, source supply-chain/localization
checks, the production build and three Axterm visual/accessibility journeys.

P-08 now has a platform-labelled `sbom:packaged` command that converts a
concrete electron-builder Resources directory into an SPDX 2.3 sidecar after
packaging. It hashes the actual ASAR, Renderer assets, unpacked files,
Axterm-provided legal material and available Electron/Chromium legal documents,
while preserving packaged manifest identities and their license declarations
without turning either into file-level conclusions. The current unsigned macOS
arm64 directory app generated a sidecar for ASAR
`055aa095fac2eb44a39d2711b0733c3f920356c0d4f44186570542d029acc74b`: it has
87 package records, 233 file records and 319 relationships. The CI matrix now
generates and uploads a required sidecar after each macOS/Windows/Linux package
build; exact invocations and the current evidence are recorded in
[IR-11 packaged-artifact SPDX sidecar evidence](evidence/IR11-PACKAGED-ARTIFACT-SBOM-2026-09-21.md).
It is a post-package sidecar so it does not perturb an installer signature or
self-referential artifact hash. This is tooling plus a local macOS result, not
Windows/Linux or installed/signed-artifact proof, a complete source-to-binary
map, or IR-02/IR-11 acceptance.

P-08 has a current unsigned macOS arm64 installer result in
[IR-13 macOS DMG evidence](evidence/IR13-MACOS-DMG-2026-09-21.md). `bun run
package` produced `Axterm-0.10.0-arm64.dmg` (141,551,782 bytes, SHA-256
`e6cf5b448fa1c29405876330408d38fe8b423d116bfb2936f27674146831694c`) and
the matching 136,220,869-byte ZIP (SHA-256
`fca349b7046ca7947cd7f08a61b7d518c00e923f9fab6d02c5597f36e41f7f60`). The
DMG journey verified, mounted, independently copied, detached and tested the
installed app. Fifteen packaged journeys passed, including the independent
system-`curl` FTP forced-PASV list/upload/download and active `--ftp-port -`
byte-exact `STOR`/`RETR` journey plus four-family Electron runtime locale
packaging; nine Windows/Linux/optional-fixture journeys skipped by condition.
The resulting 87-package/233-file packaged SPDX sidecar
(SHA-256 `fae6092a590c00f5cfebbdecbca2ee5560d2e8653cdfff14dc9d2b6928a5febf`) and
top-level SHA-256 manifest
(`96efc899adef36eb5b142c32124bbd1914f20924f1950324db7f68796fb17fd3`) record
those exact bytes. This is a fresh unsigned local macOS installation result,
not Apple signing/notarization, an authentic public update feed, a
historical-binary upgrade, Windows/Linux validation or IR-13 acceptance; IR-13
remains **Open**.

The current P-07 packaged-brand change has also been revalidated through the
actual P-08 DMG installation gate rather than only a directory `.app`. The
2026-09-22 unsigned macOS arm64 DMG (141,628,390 bytes, SHA-256
`a452ae54e27f887f81e097920824df2087ff6a46cd02d48ddf55189031da260e`)
passed `hdiutil verify`, read-only mount, independent `ditto` copy, detach and
all 15 applicable packaged journeys. The installed Phase 12 flow requires the
activity brand slot to be empty and the tab-number computed background to be
mint `rgb(47, 199, 161)`; the matching ZIP also passes `unzip -tq`. The refreshed
release-byte manifest and 87-package/233-file/319-relationship packaged SPDX
sidecar have SHA-256
`6d9f5e1a30684ac6fbd7d84490ac101134657f1e74a08f5e79ae4ef6b19cc5ab`
and `e433846401849fe7ca542ef60a2baa7c0b9fe3e80011b9d987865b42a3967b68`,
respectively. Exact artifact and blockmap hashes are appended to
[IR-13 macOS DMG evidence](evidence/IR13-MACOS-DMG-2026-09-21.md). This closes
the current unsigned macOS installed-brand regression gap, not signing,
notarization, public update service, historical-binary upgrade, Windows/Linux
installation or IR-13 acceptance.

P-08 now also makes the release-service policy gap mechanically explicit.
Root `PRIVACY.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `SUPPORT.md` describe only the current
technical and pre-release boundaries; they deliberately do not invent a privacy
controller, public security contact or staffed support SLA. The separate
[`RELEASE_SERVICE_RECORD.json`](../../compliance/RELEASE_SERVICE_RECORD.json) is
`pending` and its normal `release:services:check` is part of the source gate.
Before public distribution, the release owner must publish the real policy and
channels, record public non-placeholder endpoints, named approval and evidence,
then pass `release:services:active-check`. This prevents a local package or a
template document from being mistaken for a service promise. It is a
release-owner handoff aid, not a privacy/legal/security/support approval; P-08,
IR-11 and IR-13 remain **In progress**, **In progress** and **Open**.

P-08 also turns the production root-license archive into a reviewable package
attribution ledger. Each of the 125 source graph entries records role-tagged,
exact package-manifest author/contributor values without reducing published
object records to a display name, a non-empty manifest `copyright` declaration
when published, plus exact root LICENSE/COPYING/NOTICE lines that begin with
`Copyright` or `©`. Eight packages have no copyright declaration and are
deliberately exposed as a review queue rather than being assigned a guessed
owner; manifest author entries do not clear that queue. The generated metadata
is copied byte-for-byte into the rebuilt unsigned macOS arm64 app and is
readable under the existing About → `THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_TEXTS.json` section.
Focused package-file and About journeys pass. This mechanical ledger narrows
the human notice review scope; it does not prove complete attribution, change
the third-party license terms, or accept IR-02/IR-11.

The eight-package queue now also has a committed
`LICENSE_ATTRIBUTION_REVIEW_LEDGER.json` hand-off with a hash of the generated
source scope. `licenses:attribution:check` runs in the default gate: it rejects
drift from that exact package list and prohibits a `pending` record from
claiming evidence. A non-pending record must name a qualified reviewer, carry
an ISO date, primary source/notice reference and note, and state an explicit
conclusion. Regeneration preserves those review fields only for an unchanged
component identity, so a dependency update cannot silently discard qualifying
work or keep a removed package in scope. All eight entries deliberately remain
pending; this is a reviewable hand-off, not IR-02/IR-11 acceptance or a legal
conclusion.

P-08 now also has `bun run licenses:attribution:reviewed-check`. It accepts
only `reviewed` entries with the existing qualified-reviewer, date, primary
evidence and conclusion requirements; `pending` and `needs-follow-up` remain
hard failures. `release:final-public:check` now composes that gate with the
current component/license inventories, reviewed product-mark and four-language
records, the active public-service record, migration-removal record and final
source audit. The current records deliberately fail this release-only path:
the command is a truthful promotion barrier, not a claim that human review,
public migration or installer validation happened locally.

P-08 now also runs a current dependency-advisory query in the three-platform
CI workflow. The configured package mirror returns `404` for Bun's advisory
bulk API, so the explicit `audit:dependencies` command uses the official npm
registry. It initially exposed GHSA-67mh-4wv8-2f99 through
`drizzle-kit@0.31.10`'s deprecated loader and `esbuild@0.18.20`. A root Bun
override now resolves every `esbuild` instance to the already-direct
`0.28.2`, above the advisory's fixed `0.25.0` line. `bun pm why esbuild`
shows a single resolved version, `bunx drizzle-kit --version` still succeeds,
and full/production audit queries now return `{}`. This mitigates the current
advisory and makes future drift visible in CI; it does not close IR-02/IR-11,
replace source/binary review, or establish a security-support channel.

The `@xterm/addon-serialize@0.14.0` missing-root-license case is now mapped
to the xterm.js commit pinned in its installed manifest. Its shipped
`SerializeAddon.ts` matches the official source at that commit byte-for-byte;
the same commit's exact MIT license text is in
`licenses/xterm-addon-serialize-LICENSE.txt`, named in the transition notice,
copied into the macOS arm64 package and readable in About. A fresh package
passes both packaged license tests, and the inventory sees 11 external legal
files. The package itself still omits a root license, so the inventory keeps
reporting that fact; the provenance mapping is recorded in
[THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT](THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT.md). The unsigned
`app.asar` SHA-256 for this build is
`2653960c6255e0ba035b19f8d9c909ec70bfb286c1d0feb6af1ac5c262991c06`.
The post-change `bun run check` passes 813 tests (one skipped), architecture,
Contract, localization, historical parity and build gates. This resolves one
identified notice gap, not IR-02/IR-11 or the full SBOM.

The packaged inventory now recognizes the legacy `licenses` metadata for
`ssh2@1.17.0` and `buildcheck@0.0.7` without guessing when multiple legacy
licenses are listed. Both packaged root LICENSE files have the same SHA-256
`d06b5d27bbbbe22c36b1fd88406b1208876e2d37d795f5b8eaed951a459a3111`;
the transition notice identifies Brian White and retains MIT permission text.
A new inventory fixture and fresh macOS arm64 package test verify the source
field, license text and package presence. The current report has 89 package
instances, 11 external legal files and no missing license **metadata**; it
still correctly reports the xterm add-on's missing package-internal root file.
The new unsigned `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`b3a609d70f4127af167f971c737f44c7c3914add8475ea8f7f184fc3e0a575c1`.
This removes two false-positive inventory gaps, not the unreviewed source or
transitive-license obligations of P-08/IR-02/IR-11.

P-02 now replaces the copied filename sanitizer with `safe-transfer-name.cjs`
and the former XMODEM engine with an Axterm-owned `xmodem.ts`. The latter is
selected by the existing terminal-transfer Adapter; its old `.cjs` source is
removed. Tests cover bidirectional transfer, CRC/checksum handshakes, split
1K frames, duplicate blocks, retransmission, EOT acknowledgment, cancellation,
path-safe output, early peer handshake and timeout cleanup. The 2026-09-20
`bun run check` passes with 761 tests (one skipped), and the rebuilt macOS
arm64 app passes the packaged notice and independent-startup Playwright tests.
The published download uses a same-directory hard link to expose only a
completed file and cannot overwrite a prior destination. At that stage,
real serial/PTY, packaged XMODEM, Windows/Linux and filesystems without
hard-link support still needed evidence. The later TRZSZ replacement below did **not** clear the
terminal-transfer source gate because ZMODEM and packaged transfer evidence
remain open.

The adapted 28-bit UID helper is now removed and the then-remaining transition
adapters use an independently written 128-bit transfer nonce with exclusive
staging-file creation. ZMODEM receives to a name-independent staging file and
publishes by no-overwrite hard link; a collision suffix is UTF-8 byte-bounded.
The TRZSZ receive writer had no `finalPath`, making completion fail. It now
stages in the same directory, publishes without replacing a destination,
propagates stream errors, and cleans canceled/error staging files. Focused
tests cover the prior completion defect, empty/binary files, path-length
limits, a late destination race, missing directory and cleanup. These are
transition safety repairs, **not** independent ZMODEM/trzsz replacement or
real-remote/packaged transfer evidence. The subsequent full `bun run check`
passed 769 tests (one skipped), and a rebuilt macOS arm64 `package:dir` app
passed checkout-independent startup plus packaged license tests. At that
point, protocol transfer inside the packaged app was still unverified.

The terminal-transfer Adapter now selects an Axterm-authored `trzsz.ts` around
the separately published `trzsz2` protocol library. The adapted
`legacy-prototype/trzsz.cjs` has been removed from the working tree. Paired-library
tests cover binary download/upload, split-marker detection, cancellation,
staged no-overwrite publication, path-free events, inactive-peer timeout and
oversized-file rejection. Download completion is emitted only after
publication; a truncated upload source fails rather than looping on EOF.
A separate Node child behind `node-pty` transferred binary data, and a
rebuilt macOS arm64 `package:dir` app passed both download and upload of a
16,388-byte binary fixture through Renderer selection, Host File Grant and the
Runtime Adapter after being copied outside the source checkout. The
post-removal `bun run check` passes
787 tests (one skipped); the packaged `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`0906d3c90fa29b4bb3b25f73f968bb48146d17ba23ea449f8d7b41a67fd3c216`.
New safe directory-mode transfer is still unsupported (the prior writer also
did not implement directories). Real remote PTY, Windows/Linux,
`trzsz2` rights review and, at that stage, independent ZMODEM kept
P-02/IR-03 **In progress**.

The Runtime Adapter now selects the Axterm-authored `zmodem.ts` state-machine
integration around the separately published `zmodem2` package. The adapted
`legacy-prototype/zmodem.cjs` and its now-unused `safe-log.cjs` have been removed from
the working tree; the Adapter and tests use neutral names. Paired-protocol
tests cover binary download/upload, CRC-checked split-header detection,
cancel, idle timeout, bounded collision names, no-overwrite staging, size
limits and path-free events. A separate Node peer over `node-pty` passes both
binary directions. A macOS arm64 `package:dir` app copied outside the checkout
passes a **sequential** download then upload of 16,388 binary bytes through
Renderer selection, Host File Grant and Runtime. That packaged journey exposed
and regressed a session-reuse defect: a completed session must be reset before
the next transfer on the same terminal. The source cleanup's `bun run check`
passes 799 tests (one skipped), architecture/Contract/localization/parity
gates and build. After cleanup, the rebuilt package again passes both the
ZMODEM and TRZSZ bidirectional Playwright transfer journeys; its `app.asar`
SHA-256 is `fc23247884cbf0de6ddb4c2579464e55d4c79bc46af285162c80bebf4db951ab`.
P-02/IR-03 still require real remote PTY, Windows/Linux transfer,
filesystem portability and `zmodem2`/`trzsz2` rights
review; neither row is accepted.

XMODEM now has a separate CRC-capable Node peer behind a real local PTY,
covering complete binary download and upload rather than an in-process pair or
handshake-only capture. The explicit terminal-context-menu actions and Host
File Grant path also pass a sequential bidirectional test in a macOS arm64
`package:dir` app copied outside the checkout. An upload source is rejected
if it is a symbolic link or not a bounded regular file, including after the
grant-selection step; a focused test guards this boundary. A fresh package
passes all three XMODEM/ZMODEM/TRZSZ bidirectional Playwright journeys; its
unsigned `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`d1c3518539433765f0af99aca6c09dac9ec4ba62ea2d7443b6a99e321a2ed8b7`.
At that stage, this closed only the local macOS packaged-transfer gap, not
P-02/IR-03's remote, cross-platform, portability or rights gates. The post-change
`bun run check` passes 802 tests (one skipped), including the Level 1
architecture and Contract gates.

The XMODEM fixture now also passes binary download and upload across an
encrypted loopback SSH connection opened by the production `Ssh2Transport`.
The SSH server grants a PTY and bridges its shell channel to an independently
running Node peer; the test checks complete file bytes and transfer completion,
and closes channel, child and server in `finally`. This closes XMODEM's real SSH/PTY regression gap,
not ZMODEM/trzsz remote operation or external-host interoperability. P-02 and
IR-03 remain **In progress**. The post-fixture `bun run check` passes
804 tests (one skipped), including architecture, Contract, localization,
parity and build gates.

The same encrypted loopback SSH/PTY fixture now passes binary download and
upload for **ZMODEM and TRZSZ** too. It uses the production `Ssh2Transport`,
an SSH server-granted shell and an independently running `node-pty` peer, with
channel/child/server cleanup. The TRZSZ upload exposed a pending 100-second
receive timer in the separately published `trzsz2@1.2.0` library: successful
data receive left its `Promise.race` timeout alive. A focused regression test
first reproduced it, then the existing Bun patch mechanism gained a minimal
ESM/CJS `finally` cleanup; the real PTY peer exits naturally afterward.
`bun install --frozen-lockfile` reapplies the patch. This is a modification to
third-party MIT code, identified in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, not a claim of
Axterm ownership. A fresh macOS arm64 `package:dir` build again passes all
three bidirectional packaged transfer journeys and its exact-license/About
test (4/4); source notice SHA-256 is
`b1ee56022afa52da2a7fb29f25fc7d71ddbe4a53f4f4051da9109329db270fa5`,
and the unsigned `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`138a40c8e7965cc6f3ec2f42ade35ee38a0c18932f68cec00fb738b821f3715e`.
The loopback SSH fixture is not an external OpenSSH interoperability test or
Windows/Linux packaged proof. Filesystem portability, independently reviewed
library rights and final distribution duties remain open; P-02/IR-03 stay
**In progress**. The post-change `bun run check` passes 812 tests (one skipped),
Level 1 architecture, 256-operation Contract, localization, historical parity
and production-build gates. Its historical parity pass is not independent-
release acceptance.

The current Docker OpenSSH revalidation is recorded in
[IR-03 macOS packaged OpenSSH evidence](evidence/IR03-MACOS-PACKAGED-OPENSSH-2026-09-21.md).
Both the source `test:ssh` and copied-outside-checkout macOS-arm64
`test:ssh:packaged` journeys passed, then fixture cleanup verified zero
labelled containers and zero fixture networks remained. The packaged SSH
journey covers Host Key approval, PTY output/search, SFTP browsing and restart
persistence; its `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`055aa095fac2eb44a39d2711b0733c3f920356c0d4f44186570542d029acc74b`.
This uses a controlled local OpenSSH fixture rather than an independently
operated external service, and does not cover remote protocol peers,
Windows/Linux packages, signing or rights review; P-02/IR-03 remain **In
progress**.

The 2026-09-22 current-artifact OpenSSH revalidation now supersedes that older
package-byte result in the same [IR-03 evidence record](evidence/IR03-MACOS-PACKAGED-OPENSSH-2026-09-21.md).
After Docker Desktop 29.6.2 became available, `bun run test:ssh:packaged`
passed the real Runtime fixture, source Electron B-12 and current packaged-app
SSH/SFTP journeys. The packaged flow covered password authentication, Host Key
approval, PTY output/search, live `/tmp` browsing and bookmark/history restart
persistence against `app.asar`
`ecc1aa6ee4f949e4ace0bf3e852ffe5e2576706eef04c6c486f3c534d0dca8a1`.
Post-run label and network queries found zero fixture containers and zero
fixture networks. This closes the optional-SSH skip for the current local
macOS directory artifact, not independently operated-host, remote transfer-peer,
Windows/Linux, signed-installer or source-rights evidence; IR-03 remains **In
progress**.

The [IR-03 external-peer availability record](evidence/IR03-EXTERNAL-TRANSFER-PEER-AVAILABILITY-2026-09-21.md)
still documents the host boundary: `rz`, `sz`, `rx`, `sx`, `rb`, `sb`, `lrz`,
`lsz`, `trz` and `tsz` are absent on the macOS arm64 verification host, and the
already-local Alpine OpenSSH fixture also lacks the peers. The corrected,
fixed-digest Debian probe now records signed-package metadata and uses the
external GPL-2.0-only `lrzsz 0.12.21-10` arm64 and `0.12.21-10+b1` amd64
packages through Docker PTY rather than relabeling a Node fixture. Its opt-in
test verifies four binary directions on both Linux architectures: `sz`/`rz`
ZMODEM and `sx`/`rx` XMODEM-1K; each package is hash-checked before the
temporary container-local installation, its transfer containers have no network
and every labelled container is verified gone after cleanup. The same test
cancels receive-side `sz` ZMODEM and `sx` XMODEM-1K transfers, verifies that
each external peer exits without a published file, and cleans it up. This
lrzsz fixture is local Docker Linux arm64/amd64 external-peer evidence only. It
does not cover TRZSZ, the remaining cancellation directions, external-peer
timeout/failure behavior, an independently operated SSH target,
platform-installed products or source-rights review, so P-02/IR-03 remain **In
progress**.

P-02 now also exercises that external GPL-2.0-only `lrzsz` peer through an
actual encrypted SSH PTY: a production `Ssh2Transport` connects to a controlled
loopback `ssh2` server whose shell bridges raw bytes to the selected no-network
Debian `sz`, `rz`, `sx` or `rx` container. The opt-in suite passes ZMODEM and
XMODEM-1K download/upload plus both cancellation directions for each fixed
Linux arm64/amd64 peer, verifies bytes and no file publication, then closes SSH
channel/transport/server and proves every labelled peer container is gone. This
is real external-peer-over-SSH interoperability evidence, but the server is an
ephemeral test fixture with a generated key rather than an independently
operated host; it is neither host-key approval, packaged-app, TRZSZ-over-SSH
nor external timeout/failure evidence. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

P-02 now also has a real external TRZSZ peer rather than only Axterm's Node
fixture: the official MIT `trzsz-go 1.2.0` Linux arm64 and amd64 assets are
downloaded to an OS-temporary directory, hash-checked before extraction, and
run as their published `tsz`/`trz` binaries through a no-network, fixed-digest
Docker PTY. Each selected-platform opt-in suite transfers 16,388-byte binary
data in both directions and verifies labelled-container cleanup. The release
assets omit their license file, so the evidence separately binds the official
v1.2.0 MIT license text and its hash. A third test cancels external `tsz`
before a destination is selected: Axterm ends its session without publishing a
file and `tsz` exits successfully, which is the upstream peer's observed
graceful-cancel semantic. It does not provide the remaining TRZSZ cancel
directions, timeout or failure tests, installed-platform evidence, an
independently operated SSH host or a decision on `trzsz2` source rights;
P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

The same official external `trzsz-go` peer now also traverses an actual
encrypted SSH PTY. A production `Ssh2Transport` connects to a controlled
loopback `ssh2` server, whose shell channel bridges raw bytes to a selected
no-network Docker container running the hash-checked `tsz` or `trz` binary.
The separately opt-in arm64 and amd64 suites each pass external `tsz` download,
Axterm upload to external `trz`, Axterm-issued `tsz` cancellation and
Axterm-issued cancellation of upload to external `trz`, with no final file
publication in either cancellation direction; every SSH channel, transport,
peer process and server is closed, and labelled-container cleanup is checked.
This extends real
external TRZSZ interoperability through the production SSH transport, but the
server remains a generated-key in-process fixture. It is not independently
operated-host or host-key-approval evidence, a packaged-application test, the
timeout/failure coverage, installed-platform evidence or a `trzsz2`
source-rights decision. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

On 2026-09-22, P-02 added controlled external-peer destination-selection
timeout evidence to the two SSH-PTY suites without adding Docker or network
requirements to the default gate. For each fixed `linux/arm64` and
`linux/amd64` peer, the `lrzsz` suite now passes twelve tests and the
`trzsz-go` suite five: after the production `Ssh2Transport` receives a real
external `sz` or `tsz` handshake, the owning ZMODEM/TRZSZ session advances its
fixed 60-second selection deadline under a controlled clock, emits
`session-timeout` and `session-end`, cancels the peer and leaves an explicitly
empty destination directory. The `lrzsz` suite also confirms that an external
`sx` peer is running before advancing XMODEM's fixed 110-second no-destination
retry window; it requires `session-error`, `session-end`, a nonzero peer exit
and the same empty-directory result. Debian `sz`/`sx` exit nonzero; upstream
`tsz` exits zero for its documented graceful interrupt behavior. All SSH
channels, transports, loopback server processes and labelled Docker containers
are still closed in `finally`. This is source-level controlled-fixture evidence
only: it does not cover a stalled or failed transfer after selection, an
independently operated SSH target, host-key approval, packaged applications,
source rights or Windows/Linux installed products. P-02/IR-03 remain **In
progress**.

P-02 now also covers one post-selection external ZMODEM transport-loss case in
the same opt-in SSH-PTY suite. After a real Debian `sz` handshake, Axterm
selects a writable destination and sends the genuine receiver response; the
fixture continues draining SSH bytes but deliberately withholds later peer
frames from the product session. Advancing the fixed 60-second peer deadline
must emit `session-timeout` and `session-end`, deactivate the session and leave
the destination directory empty. A unique Docker label is polled for up to two
seconds after fixture closure, so an unreachable peer cannot leave a test
container behind. This is only controlled incoming-data-loss cleanup evidence;
it does not prove peer exit, malformed-frame handling, partial-file failure,
XMODEM/TRZSZ post-selection failure, independently operated SSH, packaged
applications or source rights. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

The same SSH-PTY suite now also waits until a selected external `sz` transfer
has written nonzero bytes to Axterm's hidden `.part` staging file, then
terminates its SSH fixture and external peer. Terminal-channel exit destroys the
ZMODEM session; the partially received staged file is removed and the selected
directory remains empty. This is controlled external-peer-exit cleanup evidence,
distinct from the already recorded incoming-data-loss timeout. It does not cover
malformed frames, non-channel-close partial-file protocol failure, XMODEM/TRZSZ
post-selection failures, independently operated SSH, packaged applications or
source rights. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

P-02 now also has a selected-destination malformed-frame cleanup regression
against the independently published `zmodem2` state machine. After Axterm has
selected the directory and successfully staged one protocol data packet, the
test corrupts a later peer data packet's CRC while keeping the terminal channel
open. It requires `transfer-error` and `session-end`, inactive session state and
an empty destination directory, proving the partly staged file is removed rather
than published. This is a deterministic source-level bad-frame boundary using a
real protocol peer, not malformed output from the external Debian `lrzsz` peer,
an independently operated host, installed application, XMODEM/TRZSZ
post-selection failure or source-rights approval. P-02/IR-03 remain **In
progress**.

P-02 now narrows one local filesystem-portability failure: ZMODEM, TRZSZ and
XMODEM all publish a verified staging file through Axterm's shared publisher.
The normal path remains a same-directory hard link, preserving atomic
no-overwrite publication. Only explicit hard-link-unsupported errors use
`COPYFILE_EXCL` as a fallback; this retains no-overwrite behavior while leaving
the staging file for the caller to remove after publication succeeds. Focused
regressions simulate an unsupported filesystem for asynchronous ZMODEM/TRZSZ
and synchronous XMODEM paths, prove bytes arrive, existing destinations are
not replaced, ordinary permission errors are propagated and no fallback source
is silently discarded. This is deterministic source-level portability evidence,
not installed exFAT/SMB/NFS or Windows/Linux release-package evidence, so the
filesystem and platform gates for P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

P-02 now also keeps XMODEM's collision fallback inside the same portable leaf-
name bound as its first publication attempt. Previously an untrusted name could
be sanitized to the full 255-byte limit and then receive a further nine-byte
random suffix after `EEXIST`; filesystems enforcing the common 255-byte leaf
limit rejected every retry even though a safe alternative name was available.
XMODEM now reserves the suffix budget through the shared sanitizer before it
constructs the same-directory candidate. The regression starts with an existing
255-byte destination, completes a real CRC-framed receive, preserves the old
file, publishes the new bytes under a distinct at-most-255-byte name, emits
completion without an error and leaves no staging file. This closes that
deterministic long-name collision defect; installed Windows, exFAT/SMB/NFS and
cross-platform package evidence remain open, so P-02/IR-03 stays **In
progress**.

P-02 also aligns upload-source revalidation across XMODEM, ZMODEM and TRZSZ.
XMODEM already rejected a selected path that became a symbolic link before the
peer handshake, but ZMODEM/TRZSZ relied only on `O_NOFOLLOW`; that flag is not
uniformly available across supported filesystems and platforms. Both asynchronous
adapters now explicitly `lstat` the selected path before opening it, retain the
`O_NOFOLLOW` request where available, and validate the opened handle as a bounded
regular file. Real `zmodem2` and `trzsz2` peer regressions replace a selected
regular path with a link to different bytes, require zero bytes to reach the
peer, require the protocol failure and session-end events, and confirm inactive
cleanup. The regression itself runs where unprivileged symbolic-link creation is
available; installed Windows and unavoidable local filesystem race/security
review remain separate evidence, so IR-03 is still **In progress**.

disposable root commit `d7afd586b593699a8768a4c4bb60fbfbff7d7a40` and distinct
clone each tracked 735 files with no submodule metadata, `vendor/legacy-prototype` or
generated OpenAPI. Frozen installation rebuilt macOS arm64 `node-pty`; the
clone's full gate passed 212 test files with five explicit skips (907 tests
with twenty-eight skips), the 368-module / 1,313-dependency Level 1 gate, 267
Contract operations, source commercialization/localization/SBOM checks and five
Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. Separately recorded opt-in tests now
make the real external `trzsz-go tsz` peer write nonzero bytes into Axterm's
selected-destination `.part` file, then close its SSH fixture; terminal-channel
exit destroys the product session, removes the staged data and publishes no
destination. This does not clear external-peer malformed frames, non-channel-
close partial-file failures, XMODEM post-selection failure, source rights,
actual exFAT/SMB/NFS, independently operated SSH, public migration, remote CI,
signing, installed Windows/Linux or owner-created repository evidence. IR-12
remains **In progress**; IR-13 and IR-14 remain **Open**.

The P-08 P-02 XMODEM staged-peer-exit candidate now supersedes that TRZSZ
candidate as local clean-checkout evidence. Its deliberate pre-evidence snapshot
had 735 files / 16,010,148 bytes / source-tree SHA-256
`d96efb07da4d618ddebe2e15c04280d19de579c17903565509a879b1d9779bf0`; its
disposable root commit `443bd4c41f5beb39f1f9752cf93a3b3dedd895e2` and distinct
clone each tracked 735 files with no submodule metadata, `vendor/legacy-prototype` or
generated OpenAPI. Frozen installation rebuilt macOS arm64 `node-pty`; the
clone's full gate passed 212 test files with five explicit skips (907 tests
with twenty-nine skips), the 368-module / 1,313-dependency Level 1 gate, 267
Contract operations, source commercialization/localization/SBOM checks and five
Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. Separately recorded opt-in tests now make

selected-destination `.part` file, then terminate only that labelled container;
the production SSH terminal channel exits nonzero, destroys the product session,

P-02 now also covers a deterministic selected-destination filesystem failure
while each protocol peer and terminal channel remain open. On POSIX, the new
XMODEM, ZMODEM and TRZSZ regressions first require nonzero bytes in the hidden
same-directory `.part` file, remove that staging pathname without closing the
already-open file, then allow the protocol to continue. XMODEM accepts a second
valid CRC block before EOT; the real `zmodem2` and `trzsz2` peers resume their
remaining bytes. Final publication must fail closed, emit the protocol's error
and session-end events, send cancellation to the live peer, deactivate the
session and leave neither a final file nor a staging name. The three focused
protocol files pass 34 tests. This is still-open-channel staging-namespace-loss
coverage, not a claim about disk-full/write-permission failure, Windows unlink
semantics, independently distributed peers, installed applications or source-
rights approval. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

P-02 now additionally exercises a true partial filesystem write followed by
deterministic `ENOSPC`, `EACCES` and `EIO` failures for XMODEM, ZMODEM and TRZSZ
while each peer or terminal channel remains open. The production default still
writes through Node's real descriptor/`FileHandle`; a narrow injected write
operation lets all nine protocol/error pairs persist exactly 64 bytes into the
real hidden `.part` file, then fail the next write. Every adapter emits its
error and session-end events, sends the protocol cancellation sequence to the
live peer, becomes inactive, publishes no destination and removes the partial
staging file. The three focused protocol files pass 43 tests; the whole
terminal-transfer directory passes 78 tests with 32 opt-in/platform skips, and
the Level 1 architecture gate remains at 368 modules / 1,313 dependencies.
This closes the deterministic source-level no-space, permission and generic-I/O
partial-write-cleanup gap, but it is not a physically full volume, real ACL/
quota/mount fault, independently distributed peer, installed package, cross-
platform or source-rights result. P-02/IR-03 remain **In progress**.

The installed `zmodem2@1.4.0` and `trzsz2@1.2.0` manifests both name ZHAO
Xudong as author, despite being separately published MIT packages without an
`@legacy-prototype` name. Their license copyright lines are now in the transition
notice. Source-rights review must determine whether retaining these packages
meets ADR-016's independent release criteria; the packages' names alone do not
settle that question. Their actual Runtime use was verified before recording
them, so they were not removed as allegedly unused dependencies.

P-06 transition UI now warns on the Legacy Prototype data import/export surface and
legacy-compatible sync Settings page that those formats/paths will be retired
only after a publicly announced migration window. It tells users to export or
back up data, notes that the data export omits secret values, and states that
remote sync data will not be silently deleted. A macOS arm64 packaged app test
verified both notices on 2026-09-20. These warnings do **not** start the public
migration clock, create the new independent export/sync format, settle the
duration, or authorize removing `legacy-prototype://` and other compatibility paths.
P-06/IR-08 remains **In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**.

The [P-06 migration coverage audit](P06_MIGRATION_COVERAGE.md) now separates
the live SQLite/Vault upgrade path, the partial legacy-compatible portable
export, the eight-category sync document, remote provider objects and deep
links. In particular, the current `_axterm` extension does **not** make that
export an independent or complete Axterm backup: non-SSH bookmarks, themes,
workspaces, triggers and secret values are among the omissions. The record
orders old-version fixtures, an independent format, exact remote-object backup,
the public migration release and only then compatibility removal. This is a
coverage audit, not new migration functionality or IR-08/IR-09 acceptance. The
legacy import preview now makes loss visible per entry: it lists mapped and
omitted fields, and explains why non-SSH bookmark records and unsupported data
sets were skipped rather than leaving them as an implicit future-parity promise.

That loss report is now derived from the actual Group, Profile and Quick
Command conversion rules instead of fixed lists. It exposes unknown top-level
properties, nested protocol paths and partially rejected step/tag arrays; a
partially converted field may appear in both mapped and omitted lists. The
Quick Command preview and commit now share the target's bounded step/tag/name
rules. An Application preview/commit regression verifies that only the selected
nested Telnet password enters the application-local Vault, and a production
Electron migration journey requires the synthetic legacy fixture's `level`,
`expanded`, `forwardAgent` and `groupId` omissions to be visible in the Chinese
UI. The fixture is not an older released binary/data export, so historical
format coverage, a public migration release/window and Windows/native-Linux
installed evidence remain open. See the
[IR-08 field-loss evidence](evidence/IR08-LEGACY-FIELD-LOSS-REPORT-2026-09-22.md).
IR-08 remains **In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**.

The migration-period `--batch-op` translator now reports what it cannot carry
forward instead of silently reducing a legacy workflow. It returns only bounded,
control-character-sanitized field paths for unsupported or shadowed connection,
parameter and command properties; values are never included, and inline
credentials remain discarded. One version-controlled synthetic workflow now
drives the parser, the source F-08 File Grant/notice journey and the copied
unsigned macOS arm64 directory-app migration journey. Those checks require the
same two translated command names and 50/0 ms delay mapping, visibly list
`workflow[0].params.password` and `workflow[1].expect`, and keep the synthetic
secret out of the parse result, notice, browser storage and SQLite. The
second-instance source regression also exposed and fixed a lost-wakeup race:
an intent arriving while the preceding queue drain was active is now latched
and replayed rather than being left unclaimed. The source second-instance case
passed three consecutive runs. The packaged journey then exposed a separate
settings race: after an opt-in configuration import, the still-open workspace
could persist its pre-import layout over the imported layout before restart.
Renderer layout persistence is now blocked from import start through the
required restart, including an uncertain commit response, while a rejected
settings import releases the block. The focused packaged migration journey
passed three consecutive runs against a 101,540,201-byte `app.asar` with
SHA-256
`def175cc7f9995102b6e4536be2379e8ceedab07f0b8ac2f220c44bb4e34c5d4`.
This is current synthetic/source/macOS-directory-package evidence, not a real
old-version workflow, signed installation, public migration release/window or
Windows/native-Linux installed evidence. See the
[IR-08 legacy batch-workflow evidence](evidence/IR08-LEGACY-BATCH-WORKFLOW-2026-09-22.md).
IR-08 remains **In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**.

The verified official Legacy Prototype v1.101.16 release uses a nine-column CSV for
`--batch-op`, an older form that the prior JSON-only migration translator did
not accept. The independent Axterm parser now handles that CSV shape for
same-bookmark command rows, drops inline passwords, reports field names only,
and refuses file-transfer actions without a File Grant. It warns that rows
which formerly opened separate sessions become one sequential Axterm
operation. A sanitized `.example.test` fixture, parser tests and source plus
packaged CLI journeys cover the conversion and secret redaction. The fixture
derives its field shape from the released app's bundled example; it is not a
user-created old workflow or a byte-identical export. This closes an
implementation-format gap only: no public migration release/window,
cross-platform installed migration or historical user workflow has been
established, so P-06/IR-08 remains **In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**.
See the private IR-08 v1.101.16 CSV evidence archive.

After adding the released CSV shape, `bun run check` passed on 2026-09-24:
220 test files passed and five conditional files were skipped (1,079 tests
passed, 33 skipped); the Level 1 architecture gate checked 358 modules and
1,307 dependencies, the Contract gate checked 267 operations, the four-locale
core catalog verified 2,578 keys, the production package-layout check passed,
and all nine visual plus two accessibility journeys passed. Locale language
and rights reviews remain pending, and this source check does not change the
IR-08 migration-release or cross-platform acceptance status.

The current P-08 local independent-build candidate supersedes the FTP

with five conditional skips (961 tests with 33 skips), the 368-module /
1,313-dependency Level 1 gate, 267 Contract operations, all source/release
hygiene checks, production layout, and five visual plus two accessibility

while capping Vitest at two workers after higher-concurrency full runs exposed

behavior and fresh-clone reproducibility, not historical-format or old-binary

**In progress**, and IR-09/IR-13/IR-14 remain **Open**. See the updated
[IR-12 evidence record](evidence/IR12-EPHEMERAL-GIT-CANDIDATE-2026-09-21.md).

The current P-08 local independent-build candidate now supersedes that

second-instance drain latch and evidence records above. Its deliberate
pre-evidence snapshot had 755 files / 15,797,670 bytes / source-tree SHA-256
`a6e0f1062276ea489943236952d09454df0df24f4c984cd29c6a1f64c64acac0`;
every snapshot category was empty. Temporary root commit

1,313-dependency Level 1 gate, 267 Contract operations, the four-catalog /

P-06 now also has a read-only remote-sync backup action on the legacy Settings
surface. It uses the Desktop Host save File Grant, copies the provider's UTF-8
sync payload without decrypting or rewriting it, writes atomically with mode
`0600` and a 24 MiB bound, and returns a SHA-256 without exposing a local path
or credential. It leaves the remote object, profile state and pending download
preview unchanged; the UI warns that unencrypted backups can contain readable
private data. Service tests cover encrypted-content identity, preview
preservation, grant rejection, missing remote, local write failure and cancel
cleanup. A source Electron test uses a real loopback WebDAV fixture plus native
save-grant flow to compare the saved file byte-for-byte with the remote UTF-8
document and confirms no remote mutation. This is a backup mechanism for the
old path, not independent export/sync conversion, a public migration release,
or cross-platform packaged proof. A separate Runtime test sends controlled
responses from each built-in HTTP adapter (GitHub, Gitee, WebDAV and Custom)
through this same save-grant operation, proving byte preservation and
header-only credentials without calling real provider accounts. IR-08 remains
**In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**. The
[IR-08 backup evidence](evidence/IR08-REMOTE-BACKUP-2026-09-20.md) records the
source and macOS arm64 packaged WebDAV backup journeys, artifact hash and open
limits.

P-06 now has an independent Axterm configuration snapshot and bounded
preview/commit importer as a first step toward replacing the old data/sync
format. Its source reads typed repositories directly, not
`Legacy PrototypeDataService`; it includes non-SSH bookmarks, hosts, profiles,
commands, custom themes, triggers and settings. Vault values and refs are
omitted; credential-dependent proxies and image backgrounds are reset with
per-field omission paths. The import preview uses a read-only File Grant,
expires after ten minutes, has a four-preview bound and reports
create/unchanged/conflict actions before any product write. Commit rechecks
the importable target-collection fingerprint and uses one SQLite transaction with persistent
source-fingerprint mappings, so collection IDs and references are remapped
deterministically and a later write failure rolls back prior writes. By default
global settings/workspaces and the application-local Vault are deliberately
preserved. A user can explicitly choose portable-settings restore in the
preview: appearance, workspace layout/named workspaces/startup/activity rail,
shortcuts, terminal, file-manager address bookmarks and monitor settings are
written in that same transaction with host/bookmark/profile/theme IDs remapped.
The live workspace remains open to avoid orphaning active sessions; its
imported layout is applied on the next full Axterm start. From the beginning of
a setting-enabled commit until that restart, current-session layout persistence
is suspended so it cannot write the still-open pre-import layout back over the
imported settings. A rejected import restores normal persistence; an uncertain
commit result stays protected because the Runtime may already have committed.
The setting-enabled fingerprint also rejects a locally changed setting before
commit.
Privacy/history choices, Vault values, credential-dependent proxies and image
bytes remain local or use their documented safe fallback; the setting option is
not a full backup.

The source configuration service test covers strict redaction, inspection,
preview/import, repeated idempotent import, opt-in portable-setting reference
remapping, local privacy/proxy preservation, setting-enabled stale-preview
rejection and a forced late constraint failure that rolls back earlier writes.
The source Electron journey consumes the version-controlled synthetic legacy
v1 data fixture through native file selection, then covers an opt-in settings
commit with zero collection writes and File Grant release. That fixture makes
the legacy File Grant/UI, credential re-entry and redaction path reproducible,
but is not evidence from a historic release or installed old binary. The
copied unsigned macOS arm64 packaged migration journey imports a one-host
independent file via the same setting choice/preview/commit flow, verifies the
Runtime SQLite result and then exercises legacy remote backup. See
[IR-08 configuration export evidence](evidence/IR08-CONFIG-EXPORT-2026-09-20.md).
Source Electron and copied-outside-checkout macOS arm64 packaged deep-link
journeys also send an identical FTP session URL through transition-period
`legacy-prototype://` and native `axterm://` second-instance ingress and verify
identical prefilled connection values; the source journey confirms the
session-only secret is not exposed to browser storage. Windows/Linux deep-link
evidence remains required before IR-08 can be accepted.
The renderer now places transition notices in one fixed, viewport-bounded
stack rather than allowing concurrent notices to overlap. The source F-08
journey first opens a legacy deep link, closes only its protocol dialog (the
serial deep-link queue deliberately does not advance while that dialog is
open), then imports a legacy command-line workflow. It verifies that both
independently dismissible notices are visible together, do not overlap and
remain inside the viewport. This is a regression guard for the public
migration-period UX, not public notice, duration, cross-platform packaged
proof or P-06 acceptance.
This is not yet a complete old-version conversion, remote sync conversion or
full data backup, so IR-08 remains **In progress** and IR-09 remains **Open**.

P-06 now also has a release-ready [migration guide](MIGRATION_GUIDE.md) that
turns the implemented Axterm configuration preview/commit, legacy-file mapping
report, remote-object backup, separate Axterm sync-profile creation and
deep-link transition into user-facing steps. It explicitly states that it is
not an announcement, requires the owner to record the public date/duration and
support channel, and forbids presenting portable files or remote sync as Vault
backups. Its recovery path preserves the old remote object/profile and never
instructs users to delete data. The guide is checked for those boundaries, but
does not replace actual old-release fixtures, public release metadata or
Windows/Linux installed-app evidence; P-06/IR-08 remains **In progress** and
IR-09 remains **Open**.

The release-owner facts that the guide requires are now held in the root
[`MIGRATION_RELEASE_RECORD.json`](../../compliance/MIGRATION_RELEASE_RECORD.json). Its
only valid current state is `pending`: `bun run migration:record:check` verifies
that it contains no accidental release claim, while the deliberately separate
`migration:record:active-check` and `migration:record:removal-check` commands
fail until a real public release, approved window, support scope and (for
removal) the approved number of stable-release milestones are recorded. The
record's validators and focused tests prevent an internal build date, future
release date or future deletion decision from being presented as a public
migration start or a completed window, and require every stable milestone to
have been published before removal approval. This is an auditable owner
handoff aid, not public metadata, a user-data test, a completed migration
window, or acceptance of P-06/IR-08 or IR-09.
The complete source gate after adding this record passed on 2026-09-21: 204
test files passed with one skipped (890 tests passed with one skipped), followed
by the 367-module/1,308-dependency Level 1 architecture gate, 267 Contract
operations, component/license/SBOM/asset/localization/snapshot checks, the
production build and five Axterm visual/accessibility journeys. This validates
the record's pending-state containment only; it is not a public release or an
acceptance of P-06/IR-08 or IR-09.

The pre-repository-replacement [IR-08 old-release fixture availability audit](evidence/IR08-OLD-RELEASE-AVAILABILITY-2026-09-21.md)
found no qualifying earlier public Axterm artifact in the current remote,
reachable history or local release directory: there are no remote tags or
GitHub Release records, and the earliest reachable source/release version is
already `0.10.0`. The only checked-in migration fixture is explicitly
synthetic. The owner must preserve sanitized old artifacts, data/sync/link/
workflow samples and hashes outside the new public tree before deleting old
history; current database rollback tests cannot be relabelled as binary-upgrade
proof. This records a concrete IR-08 evidence gap while keeping P-06/IR-08 **In
progress** and IR-09 **Open**.

The packaged prior-schema regression now also covers the legacy sync profile
that was missing from its local upgrade assertion. It starts the copied macOS
app with an explicitly pre-format `sync_profiles` table, then verifies that
migrations 36 and 37 retain its WebDAV endpoint, remote object name, selected
categories, schedule and both application-local Vault references while marking
the rebuilt row `legacy-legacy-prototype-v1`. The fixture also preserves its Host,
Quick Command, retired built-in theme mapping and three decryptable Vault
secrets, none of whose values occur in SQLite. It is a stronger packaged
prior-schema regression, **not** a historic Axterm binary or a public
migration-release proof; the old-release availability audit and IR-08 limits
remain unchanged.

P-05 now replaces the 310-entry generated upstream theme snapshot and its
generator with four Axterm-authored built-ins shared by Runtime and Renderer.
The formerly copied `Default light` terminal colors and both copied default UI
palettes are gone. Migration 34 maps retired built-in IDs in the global
terminal visual, current layout and named workspaces to the stable Axterm
Default while preserving user-created theme rows and IDs, including a
deliberate legacy-range collision. For existing databases it checkpoints WAL,
creates a non-overwriting pre-migration backup, and updates settings in one
transaction. The unit migration test verifies the backup's old theme ID and
idempotent restart. Both H-03/H-04 production Electron journeys pass with the
new catalog, including clone/edit/delete, file round trip, session/global
application and background persistence. The exact fallback and pre-upgrade
clone/export advice are in [THEME_CATALOG_MIGRATION](THEME_CATALOG_MIGRATION.md).
P-05/IR-06 remains in progress until installed cross-platform upgrade, visual/a11y, release
communication and rights review are complete. Legacy theme file import/export
is still present for the public compatibility window, visibly labeled as legacy
and deprecated in favor of Axterm configuration export for durable backup and
transfer. The source H-03 Electron journey checks the notice alongside a
complete legacy file export/import round trip; the copied-outside-checkout
macOS arm64 prior-schema package fixture checks the same notice while proving
migration 34, its backup and Vault preservation. This does not replace the
installed old-binary or Windows/Linux P-05 evidence still required for IR-06.
The default Axterm keyboard/accessibility journey also opens that Theme
workspace, verifies the notice's semantic `note` role and message, and re-runs
the visible-control/focus-order audit there. This closes only a source-Electron
accessibility regression gap for the migration UI, not P-05 or IR-10 review.

On 2026-09-21, the new-theme editor stopped directing users to the upstream
theme website. The curated Axterm built-ins, clone, import and explicit
AI-draft flows remain; a focused provenance assertion prevents that URL from
returning to the Renderer. This removes an active upstream product dependency,
not the remaining P-05 rights, upgrade or visual-review gates. The subsequent
complete source gate passed 840 unit tests (one skipped), the Level 1
architecture gate, 262 Contract operations, the production build and three
visual/accessibility journeys.
The macOS arm64 `package:dir` upgrade fixture now also starts with an old
`…0312` global theme selection, verifies migration 34 in the packaged Runtime,
checks the new terminal palette and pre-migration backup, and preserves the
old profile's host, Quick Command and Vault secret. This is a packaged
prior-schema fixture, not an installed old-binary-to-new-binary upgrade or
Windows/Linux evidence; those P-05/IR-06 gates remain open.

P-03 now removes the direct `@legacy-prototype/ftp-srv@1.0.5` dependency and its
Adapter. `NodeLocalFtpServer` implements the FTP control and passive-data
paths independently from RFC 959/2428/3659: memory-only password or anonymous
login, bounded clients/commands/passive range/listings/timeouts, confined
read/write paths, streaming transfers with root-anchored private staging and no
process-global signal handlers. Active `PORT`/`EPRT` endpoints are accepted only
when they match the control connection's peer address and use a non-privileged
port, avoiding the cross-host FTP bounce/SSRF route. Focused real
`basic-ftp` tests cover login denial, list, upload/download, symlink escape,
rename/delete, anonymous access, passive range, same-peer active LIST,
cross-host active denial, interrupted-upload cleanup,
listener counts, oversize control input and stop/restart. The source Electron
G-11 journey passes. A macOS arm64 packaged app copied outside the checkout
also started the FTP Widget, listed and uploaded through a real client, stopped
it, and exposed the revised notice. P-03/IR-04 remains in progress pending
Windows/Linux packaged protocol tests, independent source/security review and
release-artifact audit.

The Local FTP Widget has a further release-safety boundary: its public Contract,
Widget form and Runtime adapter accept only `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` or `::1`.
The shared all-interface choices were removed because this independently
implemented FTP server deliberately has no TLS, so exposing its password and
file bytes to a network would not meet Axterm's commercial security posture.
The source adapter repeats the Contract check before opening a control or
passive listener, and focused contract, adapter and source/packaged Electron
journeys require the two IPv4/IPv6 loopback choices. Encrypted network sharing
continues to be available through the SSH Server Widget. This is a source and
user-interface hardening change, not an independent FTP security review,
Windows/Linux installed-package proof, source-rights decision or IR-04
acceptance.

The accepted `::1` path now has a real IPv6 socket regression rather than only
Contract/UI enumeration. An authenticated `basic-ftp` peer connects to the
independent server on IPv6 loopback, rejects a wrong password, requires the
IPv6-incompatible `PASV` command to return `522`, confirms `EPSV` allocates only
inside the configured bounded range, lists and uploads real bytes, and checks
that the advertised local URL brackets the IPv6 literal. The same authenticated
control connection rejects an IPv4-family `EPRT` target, then accepts an
IPv6-family `EPRT` only for its own `::1` peer and completes a real active-mode
`NLST` over that separately accepted socket. After `stop()`, a new connection
to the same control port must be refused. This proves the source Runtime's IPv6
loopback authentication, passive/active-data and listener-cleanup paths on the
current macOS host; it is not Windows/Linux installed-app evidence or an
independent protocol/security or source-rights approval, so P-03/IR-04 remains
**In progress**.

The independent FTP client Adapter now rejects NUL, carriage-return and
line-feed characters in every remote-path operation before it constructs an
FTP command or starts a transfer. This covers navigation, metadata, mutation,
rename/replace, raw `MKD`/`RMD`/`SITE CHMOD`, and both streaming directions;
the focused regression verifies that no mocked client operation is reached.
Promise-returning operations reject consistently, while stream construction
fails synchronously before a stream exists. This narrows an FTP control-channel
injection boundary without changing normal path semantics. It is source-level
P-03 security evidence, not a substitute for the independent review,
Windows/Linux package fixtures, or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 now also distinguishes a normally completed FTP data stream from a
caller-cancelled one. A completed upload/download leaves the serialized
`basic-ftp` control client usable for the next operation; a `destroy()` before
the transfer settles closes that client so its data channel and queued command
cannot wait indefinitely for a still-sending peer. Focused regressions cover
error-free cancellation for both streaming directions and assert that ordinary
successful streams do not close the client. This is source-level resource
cleanup evidence, not a real remote-server failure matrix, independent
security review, Windows/Linux packaged proof or IR-04 acceptance.

The local FTP Widget's session-cap boundary now has a direct raw-TCP regression:
it keeps sixteen greeting-complete control peers open, requires the seventeenth
connection to close without acquiring a session, then disconnects one retained
peer and requires a replacement to receive a normal FTP greeting. This checks
both the `MAX_CLIENTS` refusal path and that a peer disconnect returns capacity
instead of leaking it. It is bounded source-level limit and cleanup evidence;
it does not replace authenticated real-client security review, Windows/Linux
packaged testing or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 now also verifies standard FTP `ABOR` semantics on a live control/data
pair instead of treating server stop as the only cancellation path. A raw,
authenticated loopback client enters PASV, starts a 32 MiB `RETR`, pauses after
the first data bytes, then sends `ABOR` through the still-live control socket.
The active data stream is destroyed before the serialized transfer completes,
the server replies `426` followed by `226`, and the same authenticated control
connection must subsequently answer `NOOP` with `200`. This proves that an
explicit client cancellation releases the data transfer without poisoning the
queued control session. It is a source-level local-server resource-cleanup
regression, not an independently operated FTP-server/security review,
Windows/Linux installed-package evidence or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 also covers the earlier cancellation boundary where a `RETR` has sent
`150` but is still awaiting its PASV data connection. The raw client sends
`ABOR` without opening that data socket and must receive `426` then `226`
without waiting for the 15-second data-connect timeout. The sole configured
PASV port must immediately accept a fresh PASV allocation, and the still
authenticated control connection must subsequently answer `NOOP` with `200`.
This proves that cancellation and server stop tear down a pending passive
listener as well as an active stream. It is a local source-level lifecycle
regression, not independent FTP-server security review, Windows/Linux installed
package evidence or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 also limits each announced PASV port to one accepted same-peer data
socket. A raw client races four loopback connection attempts against a one-port
PASV listener; at most one socket remains open and that retained socket must
complete a real `RETR`. The others are destroyed rather than being left as
untracked accepted sockets after the listener stops accepting. This is bounded
local resource-containment evidence, not a substitute for independent FTP
security review, Windows/Linux installed-package evidence or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 now also binds filesystem mutations to the entries selected before a data
or rename operation. `STOR` uses a mode-`0600`, root-anchored UUID staging file
whose private namespace is neither listed nor addressable over FTP; immediately
before publication it requires both the canonical target path and the parent
directory's `dev`/`ino` identity to remain unchanged. A POSIX regression holds
a real upload open after staging nonzero bytes, moves its destination directory,
replaces that name with an in-root symlink and resumes the transfer: the server
returns failure, publishes nowhere, removes the staged bytes and still answers
`NOOP`. `RNFR`/`RNTO` now similarly records and revalidates the canonical source
identity, so replacing a selected source pathname produces `550` without moving
the original or replacement. The focused server file passes 15 tests. The
[filesystem-identity record](evidence/IR04-FTP-FILESYSTEM-IDENTITY-2026-09-22.md)
also records the deliberate cross-filesystem safe failure and the remaining
path-based final-rename interval. This narrows source-level TOCTOU and partial-
data exposure; it is not independent security review, Windows/native-Linux
installed evidence, rights approval or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03's restart support now follows the RFC 3659 STREAM command sequence rather
than retaining a hidden offset after a failed transfer. `FEAT` advertises the
required `REST STREAM` line; the immediately following `RETR` or `STOR` attempt
consumes the marker before validation, invalid syntax clears it, and an
intervening command deliberately discards it. Resumed `STOR` streams the
existing prefix plus new bytes into the same private root-anchored staging
boundary, then requires the original file and destination-parent identities to
remain unchanged before atomic publication. Raw control/data regressions prove
failed and intervening-command offsets do not affect later downloads, exact
resumed `RETR`, out-of-range `STOR` rejection followed by a full upload, exact
resumed `STOR`, and fail-closed behavior when its source is replaced while the
data socket remains open. The focused server suite passes 17 tests. The
[REST STREAM record](evidence/IR04-FTP-REST-STREAM-2026-09-22.md) documents the
RFC sources and remaining path-based final-operation interval. This advances
protocol/state and atomic-publication evidence, not independent review,
Windows/native-Linux installed proof, source rights or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 now also closes two control-protocol conformance gaps in the independent
server. Because it implements `MDTM` and `SIZE`, `FEAT` declares each exactly
once using the mandatory one-space feature indentation from RFC 3659. An
`RNFR` selection is now valid only for the immediately following `RNTO`: any
intervening command clears it before processing, while `RNTO` itself still
consumes the state before validating the destination. A real authenticated raw
control connection proves that `RNFR` → `NOOP` → `RNTO` returns `503` without
moving the source, then adjacent `RNFR`/`RNTO` renames the byte-exact file and
leaves the session usable. The `FEAT` fixture requires exactly one ` MDTM` and
` SIZE` line. The focused server suite passes 18 tests; the
[feature/rename-sequence record](evidence/IR04-FTP-FEATURE-RENAME-SEQUENCE-2026-09-22.md)
records the normative sources and remaining gates. This advances local
protocol-state evidence, not independent review, Windows/native-Linux
installed proof, source rights or IR-04 acceptance.

P-03 now also has deterministic partial-write cleanup evidence for both
ordinary `STOR` and `REST STOR`. A narrow staging-stream factory preserves the
production Node writer by default; six raw TCP regressions inject `ENOSPC`,
`EACCES` and `EIO` only after 64 real bytes reach the hidden root-anchored
`.part` file. Each transfer returns `426` after `150`, removes the stage and
keeps the authenticated control connection usable. Ordinary uploads publish
no target; resumed uploads leave the original target byte-for-byte unchanged.
The focused server suite passes 24 tests. The deliberate pre-evidence
independent-source candidate passed with 762 files / 16,486,371 bytes / tree
SHA-256 `82ed1a3de4bc2de83877c4734123fe76735a081e37e2d9be8f90ee6e8525001b`,
temporary commit `55a2f430810b98dc3ba8fb3201676918c1189e7f`, 981 passing
tests with 33 skips, 368 architecture modules / 1,314 dependencies, 267
Contract operations, build and nine visual/accessibility journeys. The
[REST STREAM evidence](evidence/IR04-FTP-REST-STREAM-2026-09-22.md) now records
this failure boundary. Physical disk/ACL/quota/mount behavior, independent
protocol/security and rights review, installed Windows/native-Linux results
and IR-04 acceptance remain open; P-03 stays **In progress**.

The current P-03 FTP default path also passes a focused packaged macOS arm64
journey after the partial-write change. `bun run package:dir` produced an
unsigned app with a 101,556,447-byte `app.asar` (SHA-256
`339af8aa667f87d106d473d4c8e30b0fe50e1e5e671d3489fd11d6b82746875e`).
The packaged test copied it outside the checkout and passed one of one with
authenticated `basic-ftp` plus system-`curl` passive and active list/binary
transfer operations and clean Widget stop. The complete current packaged suite
passed 17 macOS-applicable journeys with ten platform/fixture skips, including
all three terminal-transfer protocols, license/About, migration, language
switching and prior-schema/Vault upgrade. This is current packaged macOS
default-writer evidence, not a signed installation, fault-injected installed
app, Windows/native-Linux proof or independent IR-04 review. See the
[FTP evidence](evidence/IR04-FTP-REST-STREAM-2026-09-22.md).

The corresponding P-08 clean candidate deliberately captured the source before
this evidence paragraph was written: 752 files / 15,739,512 bytes / tree
SHA-256 `60792bf2057c2e76a7be3ad12f03822b4086a0b4a6e2a6bd7f0fd6e68e56276e`.
Temporary root commit `36c7bb3cd10286c1d7b861db89e3c4b255af91e2` and a
distinct `git clone --no-local` each tracked 752 files without `.gitmodules`,
the upstream submodule, generated OpenAPI, dependencies or release outputs.
Frozen installation installed 609 packages and rebuilt macOS arm64 `node-pty`;
the clone passed 217 test files with five skipped files (960 tests with 33
skips), the 368-module / 1,313-dependency Level 1 gate, 267 Contract operations,
all source/release hygiene checks, production build/layout, and five visual plus
two accessibility Electron journeys. The candidate command removed its
temporary directories. This proves local clean-clone reproducibility of the
feature/rename-sequence change, not independent FTP review, remote CI,
Windows/native-Linux installed evidence, signed distribution, migration
removal or repository ownership. IR-04 and IR-12 remain **In progress**;
IR-13 and IR-14 remain **Open**.

P-03 now also has an interoperability regression against the operating system
`curl` FTP implementation rather than only the JavaScript `basic-ftp` client.
The bounded loopback server is exercised through real TCP with forced-PASV
login, listing, binary upload/download and wrong-password rejection, then
through active `--ftp-port -` byte-exact upload/download. The active path
exercises the server's same-control-peer `PORT`/`EPRT` `STOR` and `RETR`
handling. The test captures at most 64 KiB of client output and gives every curl
operation a ten-second deadline, so a failed external client cannot retain
arbitrary data or hang the source gate. This is source-level macOS/Linux
evidence for the independently implemented server—not Windows packaged proof,
a protocol-security review, rights approval or IR-04 acceptance.

The same system-`curl` path is included in the copied packaged FTP Widget
journey and was re-run from the current unsigned macOS arm64 DMG after checksum
verification, temporary mount, independent copy and detach. It exercises
forced-PASV listing/upload/download plus active `--ftp-port -` byte-exact
`STOR`/`RETR` against the server inside the installed app; 15 applicable
packaged journeys passed and nine Windows/Linux or optional SSH-fixture
journeys skipped by condition. This is local unsigned macOS installation
evidence only, so P-03/IR-04 remains **In progress**.

P-03 additionally has repeatable Linux arm64 and x64 **source-runtime** FTP
smokes: `bun run test:ftp:linux-container` and
`AXTERM_LINUX_FTP_TEST_PLATFORM=linux/amd64 bun run test:ftp:linux-container`
start `NodeLocalFtpServer` in a platform-selected local Bun container and
verify the matching Node architecture, PASV listing, 8,196-byte binary
upload/download and wrong-password rejection. The same real Linux TCP script
also requires a pending `PASV` `RETR` to return `426`/`226` after `ABOR` within
five seconds, proves the released listener accepts a fresh PASV allocation and
keeps `NOOP` usable, then races four same-peer connections and retains only one
for a real `RETR`. The container uses a read-only root/worktree, `--network
none`, no-new-privileges and a bounded temporary filesystem. The [IR-04 Linux
source-runtime record](evidence/IR04-LINUX-ARM64-CONTAINER-2026-09-21.md)
states its actual commands and constraints. This is not a packaged or installed
application, native-module/updater/signing evidence, an independent system
client or source/security review, so P-03/IR-04 remains **In progress**.

P-03 also closed a client-side FTP text-save data-loss path: `replace` no longer
deletes the existing destination before `RNFR`/`RNTO`. A permanent server
refusal now maps to unavailable safe replacement, leaving the original file
untouched while the application removes only its staged temporary file. The
own FTP Widget server supports direct overwrite of regular files inside its
grant after source/target/parent identity checks, but still rejects symlink
and directory targets. A three-file focused suite passed **34/34**, including
mocked refusal, real TCP `550` refusal, own-server successful replacement,
symlink rejection and real text-save-service cleanup. This is a data-preservation fix,
not proof of universal FTP atomicity, Windows/native-Linux filesystem behavior
or independent security sign-off. IR-04 remains **In progress**; see the
[FTP replacement evidence](evidence/IR04-FTP-REST-STREAM-2026-09-22.md).
An isolated unsigned macOS arm64 directory app then passed the focused

**18/28** with ten conditional skips. Its `app.asar` SHA-256 is
`7c5920f6bf9cad35ad3922940eeea0ca8d131ecb4cbb1cca073ef9b1c6b662f7`;
the old `release/` application was not overwritten. This remains local
engineering evidence, not a signed/cross-platform IR-04 acceptance.

The Linux arm64 and emulated x64 source-runtime FTP smokes now exercise that
own-server `RNFR`/`RNTO` overwrite over real TCP as well: regular-file
replacement succeeds without a delete-first step, while an in-root symlink
target returns `550` and leaves both the original and rejected stage intact.
Both `bun run test:ftp:linux-container` and its `linux/amd64` variant passed
on 2026-09-23, as did the focused script test. This is not native installed
Linux, third-party-server or independent security/rights evidence, so IR-04
remains **In progress**. See the
[Linux FTP source-runtime record](evidence/IR04-LINUX-ARM64-CONTAINER-2026-09-21.md).
Both constrained container commands passed again against the current worktree
on 2026-09-24, including rename-overwrite/symlink preservation and PASV
ABOR/single-peer lifecycle checks. This refreshes only Linux source-runtime

The P-07 1280×800 data-migration screenshot was deliberately refreshed after

The copied filename/nonce helpers and former XMODEM/ZMODEM/trzsz adaptation layers,
